# Migration-spec coverage

This is an evidence ledger for the full [Wayfinder destination](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/1), not a completion claim. A research inventory, a throwaway demonstration and an accepted design answer are different evidence. Runtime gates described in a spec remain future work unless a linked observation actually covers them.

The capability baseline is the [wx UI inventory](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/blob/6f91fd28eab4c42d2c00f848606b47585c2b6bc8/docs/research/ui-inventory.md), including its complete action/menu/control appendices. The table below tracks surface families; it does not replace that detailed parity list. No absent prototype implies permission to drop a capability.

## Destination artifacts

| Requirement | Current authoritative evidence | What remains before the map can finish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Architecture ADRs | Accepted foundation, platform, compatibility, build, media/audio, font, document/time, distribution and backend contracts in [ADRs](../adr/); [architecture](architecture.md); [backend ownership](backends.md) | Native player/docking feasibility, detailed automation and lifecycle choices; responsibilities must agree across modules. Backend ownership and isolated FFMS2 qualification direction are accepted, not production-qualified. |
| Reviewed runnable mockups and written UX for every surface | [Visual language](ux/visual-language.md), accepted [workspace/shell contract](ux/workspaces.md), [grid direction](ux/subtitle-grid.md); published detailed HTML studies below and bounded native experiments | The [reviewed surface layouts](ux/reviewed-surface-layouts.md) record twelve named placement answers. Remaining behavior/depth and native gates stay separate; representative controls do not sign off every workflow. |
| Rewritten core design | Accepted [document model](document-model.md), [time semantics](time-semantics.md), [backend resource contracts](backends.md), module ownership and five named transaction/lifecycle corrections | Remaining [draft/command/undo](proposals/edit-transactions.md) and [Session/recovery](proposals/application-lifecycle.md) policies. Existing source tests were inventoried, not executed as rewrite proof. |
| Stateless CMake build | Accepted [CMake workflow/vcpkg/Qt contract](build.md), installed latest-MSVC/Qt slice and actual Linux host/metadata preflight; platform policy unchanged | Linux is proved from an empty cache on Ubuntu and Fedora (B1, B2-U, B2-F; see [implementation evidence](#implementation-evidence-wave-1)). The Windows lock, latest-MSVC slice and installer behavior (B2-W) remain. |
| Agent-sized implementation plan | [Conditional sequence proposal](proposals/implementation-sequence.md), tracked by [Sequence the Qt rewrite into evidence-gated implementation issues](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/65) | Sequence actual implementation issues with input contracts, deliverables, meaningful acceptance evidence and dependencies after design decisions settle. |
| Carry-over and parity | Accepted [compatibility contract](compatibility.md) and [fourteen named departures](compatibility-decisions.md); [data inventory](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/blob/1eea34cd5db8db276f2e71cdff3f43f345cefd5b/docs/research/data-inventory.md), [core inventory](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/blob/5d392a10c98b97c9b2b92f4b3f9dc49cd429752c/docs/research/core-inventory.md) | Individual defect dispositions, concrete fixture ledger and per-operation loss/error rules. Preserve files, catalogs, sessions/autosaves and unchanged Lua scripts; one-shot settings/hotkeys import excludes themes. |
| Shared vocabulary | Accepted domain glossary in the single root [CONTEXT.md](../../CONTEXT.md) | Keep later contracts consistent; add genuinely new domain terms through review. Terminology acceptance does not decide format/API semantics. |
| Measurable verification and release gates | Accepted [performance](performance.md), [platform](platform-policy.md), [distribution](distribution.md), [testing](testing.md) and [beta/rollback/cutover](proposals/cutover-readiness.md) policies; limited native observations | Native input/accessibility execution, reference-host bindings and deployment proof. Bounded opt-in beta policy is accepted; all stable gates and concrete owner approval remain required. HTML checks and Windows synthetic samples are insufficient for platform-wide claims. |

## Surface evidence

Status refreshed on 2026-09-27. **Published / awaiting reaction** means a runnable artifact and coverage notes exist, not that its proposed interaction or behavior is accepted. **Partly accepted** records only the named outcome; other questions stay open. The latest GitHub replies accept the named placements in [reviewed surface layouts](ux/reviewed-surface-layouts.md), including the Line editor local inspector and Conversion staged review. Those placement questions are answered; remaining behavior is not a repeat layout review. Each study's coverage notes distinguish working representatives from remaining depth; no missing case approves a capability drop.

The [surface decision routing worksheet](proposals/surface-decision-routing.md) refines the broad remaining-review bullets below: shared policy is answered once, compatible legacy behavior is characterized and preserved, and only named departures or genuinely new interactions require a further vote. C07/C43 and the three L58 outcomes are accepted; draft navigation and remaining shared policies are not implied by those approvals.

| Surface family | Current design evidence and review status | Remaining review depth and verification |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Application shell, tabs, workspaces and Home | Accepted Workspace per Document tab (ADR 0020, replacing the shared one), Classic controls/status, named tool/dialog grouping, optional task presets and protected comparison; compact Home with task dialogs accepted in the [file study][files] | Native docking/persistence qualification and detailed task transitions remain open; the [docking architecture](docking.md) is accepted. Home layout acceptance cannot approve lifecycle or recovery behavior. |
| File operations, recents, Sessions and recovery entry | [File/Session study][files] compact Home/task-dialog placement accepted: staged open, save/export outcomes, external changes, multi-close and partial restore; [lifecycle proposal](proposals/application-lifecycle.md) unaccepted | Set pending-work and collision policies, partial restore/omission and uncertainty. Full codecs/encodings, single-tab close, path aliases, concurrent writes and native recovery still need fixtures; modeled files are not durability proof. |
| Subtitle grid and row operations | Painted direction and [detailed Grid UX](ux/grid-operations.md) accepted, including toolbar/task review and J56 selected-only Join; [C++ table/cell experiment][grid-native] matched 22 bounded in-process observations with both installed Qt kits | Implement and characterize the full command/clipboard/split/join/sort/column families under the written obligations. Shared draft/history policies stay with edit transactions. Announcement policy accepted; [native Grid](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/45) closed with named G45 accessibility/input/cache/performance obligations transferred as required evidence. |
| Grid filtering and Line groups | [Detailed Grid UX](ux/grid-operations.md) preserves inclusive ranges/hidden anchors and retained selection; G56 actual-group-break rejection with explicit removal is accepted | Verify full filter truth tables, flags, cross-hidden creation, grouping commands, shortcuts and Undo. Imported malformed flags stay source-preserving data; no automatic repair or broad runtime pass is inferred. |
| ASS editor and Line properties | [Mapped editable ASS approach](ux/ass-editor.md) accepted, including Enter commit/advance outside IME; [native mapped editor][editor-native] has bounded evidence. [Line editor study][editor-ui] local inspector is accepted under [Prototype Line editor metadata, ASS controls and completion workflows](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/63) | Complete metadata, wrapping/insertion, completion, spelling and active-text-role behavior. After-tag insertion and retained intervening tokens are the accepted boundary defaults; draft history stays with edit transactions. Named E28 obligations (IME, RTL, accessibility, grammar/Style-aware restoration, undo, shortcuts, performance) transferred to the implementation sequence as required evidence, not passes. |
| Translation and two-Document comparison | [Study][translation] partly accepted: [stacked fields, bottom reference and independent navigation](ux/translation-comparison.md); [translation operation companion][translation-operations] published / awaiting reaction | Captured tag/copy/paste/alignment reviews now expose representative row and timing changes. Role protection, bulk-copy, draft reconciliation, confirmation traversal, matching/synchronization and export/fallback policies await reaction and exact legacy fixtures. Accepted placement does not approve the representative commit or matching algorithm. |
| Audio timing and karaoke | PortAudio/QSG direction accepted; [audio study][audio] local strips accepted | Review audition windows, timing selection versus commit, advance, snapping, gains/zoom, syllable split/join and numeric alternatives. Native waveform/spectrum, output/clock accuracy and complete karaoke/tag cases remain separate evidence. |
| Video, transport and fullscreen | Active-mode ownership accepted; [video study][video] side rail accepted; [Windows native transport][media-native] supplies bounded observations | Review general/indexed controls, stream/chapter selection, capture/file actions, coordinates and fullscreen arrangement. Handoff clock/mode/error behavior accepted; [media handoff](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/50) closed with M50 obligations transferred. Real source PCM, device-tail acknowledgement, GPU/libass/color, Linux and complete player/filter/monitor parity remain required evidence. |
| Visual typesetting tools | [Video study][video] side rail accepted, with all eleven visual families represented | Set draft/scope/commit/cancel behavior and coordinate feedback. Complete ASS geometry, transform/clip/drawing parsing, style inheritance, presets and numeric/keyboard parity remain explicitly limited by the study ledger. |
| Styles and external catalogs | [Styles study][styles] parallel Document/catalog lists accepted: separate Document/catalog targets, drafts, transfer conflicts, rename and cleanup | Review organization and conflict/reference policies; verify full Style/tag behavior, catalog persistence, names/order and loss cases. Illustrative previews are not libass or font identity evidence. |
| Fonts, profiles and colors | [Styles study][styles] parallel Document/catalog lists accepted. [Font contract](fonts.md) and narrow diagnostic hook accepted; [Windows experiment][font-native] retains incomplete fallback reimport, with [resolver-loss diagnosis][font-diagnostic] | Review picker/search/catalog organization and alpha/recent/screen-picker actions. Fallback-dependent glyphs are reported, with no portable replay promise (accepted). [Font identity](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/47) closed with F47 fontconfig/variable/refresh/ABI/corpus obligations transferred; actual screen capture remains with the Styles surface. |
| Find/replace, results and select-lines | [Search study][search] scope rail accepted: expression/scopes, frozen results, checked replacements, protection and mock external files | Review scope/history behavior. Native wx-compatible expression semantics, real files/encodings, all selection modes and command/draft collisions need fixtures; JavaScript matching is not parity. |
| Minor-error correction and spelling review | [Search study][search] scope rail accepted: authored words, correction rules/order and preview | Review rule and word workflows; keep original-offset/sequential differences as unapproved candidates. Real dictionaries/languages, persisted rules/words, complete replacement semantics and UI/native spelling integration remain. |
| Time shift and timing postprocessor | [Timing study][timing] dock placement accepted; accepted [time semantics](time-semantics.md) remains authoritative | Review subsets, anchoring, profiles, keyframe windows and preview/apply. Complete timed-tag/end correction, continuity boundaries, keyframe traversal/protection and unavailable-timebase cases need native fixtures; no new timing fix follows from layout approval. |
| Script properties, resample and Conversion | [Utilities study][utilities] awaiting placement review; [Conversion companion][conversion] staged review accepted under the same [Prototype Script properties, resampling, Font collector and extraction dialogs](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/60) | Review resolution metadata versus coordinate resampling and revision-bound loss approval. The companion exposes ASS/SRT/MicroDVD/MPL2/TMPlayer options, FPS/style/catalog selection, duration-per-letter and tag insertion. Exact serializer/encoding/TLMode, complete format-pair fixtures and named defect dispositions remain unproved. |
| Font collector and MKV extraction | [Utilities study][utilities] published / awaiting reaction: captured targets, incomplete collection, destinations, staging/cancellation and results | Review partial-output approval, collisions and extraction activation; verify actual used-font/glyph analysis, selected bytes/reimport, track/attachment extraction, filesystem permissions and native cancellation. |
| Automation manager, script dialogs and progress | Separate Lua process and persistent state per loaded script accepted; [automation study][automation] persistent manager tool accepted; [native isolation evidence][lua-isolation] records 16 bounded Windows offscreen scenarios | Per-script processes, one active macro and explicit restart/no rerun are accepted; remaining modal focus, cancellation escalation, reservation and transaction behavior stay open. Full unchanged scripts, native modules/DependencyControl, all dynamic-control semantics, staged publication and production IPC remain gates; Lua stubs/validation/button quirks await named decisions. Typed native IPC and one unchanged small macro are observed, not complete host qualification. |
| Settings, shortcut editor and importer | [Settings study][settings] page task dialog accepted; [mechanics proposal](proposals/settings-import.md) and [207-option / 243-action worksheet](proposals/settings-migration-map.md) unaccepted | Review conflict routing, import/repeat/rollback and unavailable values. Enumerated destinations and routes now exist; contexts, interruption recovery, external paths/associations and preserved authored collections need native fixtures. Legacy themes remain excluded. |
| History | [Detailed Grid UX](ux/grid-operations.md) accepted; persistent follow/pin tool and C43 saved identity settled | Remaining draft collisions, grouping/coalescing, restored context and filter/view-history treatment belong to [edit transactions](proposals/edit-transactions.md). Implement real history/navigation and production publication; the HTML is not runtime evidence. |
| Autosave browser and cleanup | [File study][files] compact Home/task-dialog placement accepted: corrupt fallback, unsaved recovery copy, exact cleanup candidates and last-valid/active guards | Decide recovery-copy and retention behavior explicitly. Real legacy snapshots versus proposed bundles, integrity checks, resource leases, capacity edge cases and actual filesystem cleanup remain unproved. |
| Logs, progress, errors, updates, Help/About | [File study][files] compact Home/task-dialog placement accepted: shared tasks/results and update/help fixtures | Review inspectability and placement; verify native ownership/cancellation, safe diagnostics, real release matching, offline/failure states, credits and notices. Reminders, reports and updates are simulated; notification-only policy remains accepted. |

## Implementation evidence (wave 1)

Status refreshed on 2026-10-02. This section records what the [wave-1 cards](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/66) have actually executed on Linux. A closed card means its listed evidence passed in CI; it does not settle the surface rows above, and obligations transferred by name stay open until their own evidence exists. Windows cards wait for the Windows build (B2-W, F2-W).

The cumulative evidence is the `qt` branch at `090e966e`: [Ubuntu 26.04](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/actions/runs/37011681361) and [Fedora 44](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/actions/runs/37011681507) ran every test below from an empty cache, and the [legacy capture](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/actions/runs/37011681370) drove the wx app at `20d647c4`.

Qt metadata is retained as release assets because upstream deleted it. |

going back to a script parsed before needs no parse), a new frame size drawn as a new renderer; `overlay_blend_tests` (8): blendAssBitmap on each path the machine has (plain, SSE2, AVX2; run-time choice) against legacy BlendAssBitmap byte for byte over every coverage and opacity and 400 sample bitmaps of every width and tail, the clearing boxes, the shared buffers (reused only when no frame holds them, four kept), the CSRI premultiply pass against the per-pixel loop it replaced and its bounds; `csri_renderer_tests` Csri.ABufferIsReusedClearedWhereItWasDrawn (the CSRI frame in a reused buffer cleared where it was drawn, its changed areas the old and new bounds). Measured on the four heavy test items (Linux, 1920x1080, host load 12-20, the last two frames held as the application holds them): render() over the heavy stretches 5.3-5.9 ms a frame before, 1.4-1.9 ms after; every frame of the heavy points and stretches byte-identical to legacy's composite (before: up to 23 levels apart); an edit's prepare() and first frame 28-72 ms before, 8-57 ms after for an edited script (the parse remains) and 2-8 ms when the script is one parsed before. a frame asked for with its subtitles (Automation's get_frame) and the style previews lay the overlay with rounded divisions as a saved snapshot does (`video_session_tests` VideoTest.AFrameWithSubtitlesHasTheOverlayLaidAsASnapshotLaysIt, `video_view_tests` VideoSnapshot.CompositeOverlayLaysItAsTheSnapshotDoes; they truncated before). libass's cache limits stay at libass's defaults: larger ones (20000 glyphs/256 MB, 40000/512 MB) gave no gain over three passes of the heavy stretches. 38c (2026-10-14, N3-script-attachments): the script's [Fonts] reach libass, CSRI and the Style editor's preview as font leases ahead of the external ones, the renderer script keeps [Graphics] and leaves [Fonts] out (`ass_attachments_tests` (5), `libass_renderer_tests` LibassRenderer.DrawsTheScriptsAttachedFonts, `csri_vsfiltermod_tests` VsfilterMod.DrawsTheScriptsAttachedImageAndFonts on Windows, `hikari_ui_shell_tests` attachedFontsReachTheLoadedVideo) | — |

`portaudio_tests`: stream, clock, restart, rescan and reopen by id, simulated loss and recovery on a desktop ALSA device and the CI null PCM; on Windows (Winix VM with an emulated HDA device and a hot-plugged QEMU USB audio device, both on a silent "none" audiodev, approved 2026-10-04) the stream tests on DirectSound (the default) and WASAPI (`audio-wasapi`), and `PortAudioHotplug` with a real unplug and replug (tools/winix/hotplug.sh): on WASAPI the stream's callbacks stop, PortAudio ends the stream about 1.9 s later without the finished callback and the owner reports DeviceLost (a stream that stops being active without a stop request is lost; start() then refuses with DeviceLost); on DirectSound the stream stays active with stalled callbacks, so the estimate becomes invalid but no DeviceLost is reported; on both the device leaves the list, its old id no longer opens, and it reopens under the name Windows gives it on return ("Speakers (2- Audio Device)") Closing evidence at 9aa897065 (closed 2026-10-15; Linux CI run 38602894573 (Ubuntu, offscreen and xvfb, 1578 passed) and 38602902711 (Fedora, 1508 passed), Windows CI run 38602887728 (empty-cache and warm-SDK jobs, 1501 passed each), Winix VM job-e80e6ea9353845ce8a56914e72ce61c4 (full test: 1498 tests, 0 failed, 8 skipped): winix audio job-cdcdf264b6364e2fa15c6fcce5993634 (23/23, DirectSound) and audio-wasapi job-797c40d9a8e84777b4c77f8625a64c78 (9/9) on the VM's HDA endpoint (silent backend); PortAudioHotplug.RealDeviceLossInvalidatesAndTheReturnedDeviceReopens with a real QEMU USB audio unplug and replug (tools/winix/hotplug.sh) on WASAPI (job-e019510a7b824d57aaa6ecac7acfa8b2) and DirectSound (job-eb7f79c00e2a4341a87e61f8e6127981): HOTPLUG-READY, LOST and RECOVERED, passed; on Linux the portaudio and audio_engine tests passed in Ubuntu and Fedora CI (null ALSA PCM). | Linux real device loss is not observed: the CI host has no card, and through PipeWire's ALSA plugin a removed sink does not end or stall the stream (checked in a private PipeWire session in a container). DirectSound reports no loss beyond the stall; on Windows hardware (H2, A11) Windows moved the DirectSound stream to the new default itself. The reopen policy is H2's (#125, decision 60, H2-output-device): the owner reopens after a rescan on a default change, a loss or a failed start, and goes on from the frame heard |

The editor's own audio output and the audio box arrive with the Audio wave. Frames are converted on the CPU off the GUI thread (2026-10-13, the playback pipeline in [media.md](media.md#playback-pipeline)): `playback_pipeline_tests` (latest-wins handoff in and out of the worker, pooled buffers, the next frame's overlay rendered ahead, overlay bounds, SIMD and scalar Y'CbCr rows bit-exact for every matrix and for DirectShow's coefficients), `video_session_tests` PlayingTest (one notification per player frame, older and pre-pause results dropped, new subtitles render the worker's frame again), `video_frame_bgra_tests` (NV12, NV21, P010, P016, YUV420P, YV12, YUV420P10, YUV422P; the frame's matrix and range; Qt's own conversion within 2 where Qt has a graphics device, xvfb: offscreen Qt converts everything as BT.601 limited), `general_player_tests` tenBitFramesAreConverted (FFV1 yuv420p10le fixture) and framesDoNotBacklogWhileTheGuiThreadIsBusy, `presenter_tests` onlyTheChangedOverlayRegionIsUploaded, anEmptyOverlayIsNotDrawn and aTextureThatCannotBeMadeFailsThePresent, `playback_workflow` (one notification per frame, texts at about 10 Hz, no visual tools or status bar notification per frame). Player frames take the indexed video's matrix and range (`video_frame_bgra_tests` theIndexedVideosMatrixApplies, `video_session_tests` the V4 matrix test), match the paused frame within one level on average for TV.709 and TV.601 scripts, and are neither rotated nor mirrored (rotationAndMirroringAreNotApplied). `playback_pipeline_tests` also covers overlays rendered ahead once per frame for 23.976 fps in milliseconds and 29.97 fps in microseconds, and withheld for a variable rate. `audio_display_tests` covers the disk cache, which is written off the GUI thread and read back before it reaches the file. Measured on Linux against a 0.2 s lag target: 35 ms of extra GUI work per frame gives a worst lag of 43 ms on Ray MV and 52 ms on ep10 (before: 2.8 s and 3.3 s). On Windows (MSVC, 2026-10-13) the DirectShow, presenter, playback workflow, BGRA, general player and pipeline suites pass, the DirectShow suite with no skips; Direct3D hardware frames stay untested because the VM has no hardware decoder. GPU conversion (2026-10-16, branch qt-gpu): player frames kept as Y'CbCr planes and converted in the presenter's shader on RHI backends with R8 and RG8 textures, the CPU conversion elsewhere: `video_frame_bgra_tests` planesConvertToTheCpuPathsBytes (the eight formats at odd and even sizes down to 3x2, byte for byte against the BGRA path) and planesTakeOneAndAHalfBytesAPixel, `DirectShowFrames` (YV12 and NV12 planes against legacy's conversion, odd sizes), `presenter_tests` ycbcrFramesShowAsTheirCpuConversion (NV12, YUV420P, YUV422P at 96x64, 95x63, 33x17 and 2x1 for BT.709 limited, BT.601 full and DirectShow's coefficients, within one level at 1:1), ycbcrFramesFollowZoomAndPlacement, aPlanarFrameShownAgainIsNotUploadedAgain, theGpuPathIsChosenByTheBackend, aPlaneThatCannotBeMadeFailsThePresent, and in the software renderer the CPU fallback; `video_session_tests` PlayerFramesAreKeptAsPlanesOnlyForAPresenterThatConvertsThem. While playing (2026-10-14, qt-playpos): the status ("Frame N of M"), the times field and the seek bar follow the player's frame, the indexed frame holding the last delivered frame's start (legacy RendererVideo::GetCurrentFrame, FrameShownAt(m_Time)), refreshed with the texts at most every 100 ms as legacy's m_VideoTimeTimer ran VideoBox::RefreshTime; before, they stayed at the frame playback started from until a pause, and the times field was empty. The seek bar's own moves seek the player, which goes on from the frame (VideoSlider::SendTime, RendererFFMS2::SetPosition), and its thumb stays under the pointer while held (legacy's `block`); following the frame never seeks. `video_session_tests` PlayingReportsThePlayersFrameAndTheSeekBarSeeksIt (the current frame from the player's frames, one player seek however many frames arrive, the seek bar while playing seeks the player and keeps playing, paused it shows the frame); `playback_workflow` reads the status, the times and the bar twice while playing, the second time once the player is 8 frames on (each read between the player's frames just before and just after it, at most 250 ms of the 24 fps fixture behind: the 100 ms pace, a frame and timer slack; the bar advancing; the player's frames recorded with the last one held, so a reused address never hides one), clicks the bar at 90 % while playing (the player's frames jump to frame 38 or later and never step back, the bar follows) and prints the GUI thread's CPU time while playing; both checks fail on the code before. GUI thread CPU while playing: the cfr fixture offscreen 2.1-2.5 % (before 2.0-2.5 %); a 1080p HEVC episode offscreen for 13 s, 16.1-17.8 % (8f6e3d27b: 16.7-16.9 %), about 7 text updates a second and one notification per frame in both. Windows (Winix VM, MSVC, 56083cf0, build job-9462d8cec770400aa1056a6d0df7dfcd): `playback_workflow` offscreen passes three times (GUI thread 12.7-17.0 %, GetThreadTimes' 15.6 ms ticks; the same source before the change 12.7-18.3 %, failing both new checks with the status, times and bar at 0 while the player was at frame 17-27), `video_session_tests` 28 passed, `video_workflow` and `video_fullscreen_workflow` pass. Linux: the 60 `hikari_ui_*` offscreen suites pass locally; on akari-rvm the other 1494 tests pass (9 skipped: PortAudio without a device, the third-party corpus) after the session test's last-frame case was corrected. Follow-ups (2026-10-14, qt-playfollow, user decisions items 30-32): (1) the seek bar dragged, docked or fullscreen, seeks at most once every 50 ms with every player and once more on the frame it is let go on, unless that frame was just sought (approved departure [V2-seek-bar-drag-throttle](compatibility-decisions.md); legacy sought per move except DirectShow, which sought on release only): `seek_throttle_tests` (3), `hikari_ui_playing_frame_tests` seekBarDragSeeksAtMostEvery50Ms and fullscreenSeekBarDragSeeksAtMostEvery50Ms (pointer drags of 60 moves 4 ms apart: 6-7 seeks in about 270 ms, the last on the frame let go on, paused and playing); (2) while playing, the features that read the video's frame read the player's (VideoSession::reportedFrame; legacy FrameShownAt(VideoBox::Tell())): the Line editor's time difference, inserting Lines timed to the video, the visual tools' time and outside-the-Line warning, Set FPS from video, Split at video time, Shift times to the video, the audio mark from the video, the tab's video position and Automation's video_position; before, they read the player frame's index -1 and did nothing, used no frame or kept position 0; Play right after Pause starts from the paused frame, not 0: `video_session_tests` TheReportedFrameIsThePlayersWhilePlaying, `hikari_ui_playing_frame_tests` featuresReadThePlayersFrameWhilePlaying (a fake general player at frame 30 while playback started from frame 2; each reader checked against frame 30's times, except Automation's video_position, which reads the same accessor untested); (3) a player time past the last frame's start is the last frame, so the status, the times field and the bar never fall back to the frame playback started from, and Pause shows it: `video_session_tests` APlayerTimePastTheLastFrameIsTheLastFrame, `hikari_ui_playing_frame_tests` thePanelReportsTheLastFrameAtTheEnd. The UI checks of (2) and (3) fail with the old frame source (shownFrame, no clamp). Linux (8fca177f6): the 61 `hikari_ui_*` offscreen suites pass locally (`hikari_ui_shell_tests` 390 s); on akari-rvm the other 1517 tests pass (9 skipped: PortAudio without a device, the third-party corpus); `playback_workflow` still reads one notification per frame, 0 visual tools notifications and the panel following the player (GUI thread 3.8-4.4 % offscreen on a loaded host, the same with the visual tools' time put back on the shown frame). CI at 8fca177f6: Windows 38551133876, Ubuntu 38551137171, Fedora 38551140807 passed; no Winix VM run. Follow-ups (2026-10-14, user decisions items 35-37), matching legacy: (4) while playing, VIDEO_5_SECONDS_FORWARD/BACKWARD, VIDEO_MINUTE_FORWARD/BACKWARD and GLOBAL_GO_TO_NEXT/PREVIOUS_KEYFRAME jump from the player's frame and playback goes on from the target (legacy Seek(Tell() + ms) and SetPosition(NextKeyframe(Tell())), RendererFFMS2::SetPosition seeking the playing video and resetting the play end); before, they moved only the requested frame, nothing was shown and the player played on; (5) while playing, next/previous frame does nothing and reports no success (legacy RendererVideo::ChangePositionByFrame returns while Playing); before, it moved the requested frame; (6) while playing, the seek bar, status and times field show a seek (a new play epoch: the bar, a jump, the active Line) and a seek-bar release at once, then follow at the 100 ms pace again; before, the bar could show the frame of the last 100 ms update for up to 100 ms after a release, and a jump not at all. `video_session_tests` JumpsWhilePlayingGoFromThePlayersFrameAndPlayOn (the jumps from the player's frame 7, 5, 8 and 9 to frames 4, 8, 4, the last and the first, each a player seek with playback going on; step refused with no request, no seek and no decode; paused, steps and jumps from the paused frame); `hikari_ui_playing_frame_tests` theBarShowsASeekWhilePlayingAtOnce (L to the last frame and ; to the first while playing, the bar on the target synchronously; Next frame refused; a drag let go while playing, the bar on the frame let go on synchronously). Each check fails on the code before (jumps: no player seek; step: true with a decode request; bar: still on frame 10 after the jump to 47). Linux (on 9aa897065): the 61 `hikari_ui_*` offscreen suites pass locally under a virtual X11 display (`hikari_ui_shell_tests` 360 s); on akari-rvm the other 1520 tests pass (9 skipped: PortAudio without a device, the third-party corpus). CI on the same change over e14280112 (50931bcec): Windows 38584857307, Ubuntu 38584864188, Fedora 38584870417 passed (Fedora on a rerun: the first attempt failed only in Spelling.DictionaryFoldersInSearchOrder, the flake 9aa897065 fixes); no Winix VM run. | Time and frame entry: legacy's times field is read-only, and so is this one. The legacy dialog answers stay source-characterized until a decision on how to observe them (the baseline cannot show one) |

| [L6](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/124) | Automation compatibility corpus: unchanged scripts, native modules, MoonScript (A33-compat) | `lua_helper_tests`: the legacy preloads through the bundled wrappers — `aegisub.re` (Boost u32regex over ICU), `aegisub.unicode` (Boost.Locale), `aegisub.lfs`, lpeg 0.10 and luabins from the unchanged legacy sources; legacy Install (trailing-separator include paths, the .moon-preferring module loader, moonscript.loadstring); .moon scripts load and their errors map to .moon lines; the legacy add_stack_trace format. Corpus (artifact `automation-corpus.json`): every bundled Autoload script loads unchanged and registers its macros, including Aegisub-Motion (MoonScript) and the DependencyControl Toolbox, whose native modules (BadMutex, PreciseTimer, DownloadManager over libcurl) build from the unchanged `Thirdparty/DependencyControl` sources into a staged automation tree Closing evidence at 9aa897065 (closed 2026-10-15; Linux CI run 38602894573 (Ubuntu, offscreen and xvfb, 1578 passed) and 38602902711 (Fedora, 1508 passed), Windows CI run 38602887728 (empty-cache and warm-SDK jobs, 1501 passed each), Winix VM job-e80e6ea9353845ce8a56914e72ce61c4 (full test: 1498 tests, 0 failed, 8 skipped): winix thirdparty job-76034ce198d34f00a4f958b52f9ee26c on the VM (the pinned corpus from the backends artifacts): LuaJitBuildMatchesTheLegacyPlatform, ThirdPartyCorpus.ScriptsLoadAsAutoloadDoes, FetchedProjectsAreAvailable, MacrosApplyToTheFixtureDocument, CaptureProbeRunsTheCorpus and automation-thirdparty-stage, 6/6; the same corpus tests passed in Ubuntu CI and Windows CI. | Quirks pinned: lfs mkdir/touch/rmdir return nil, nil on success (lfs.moon applies tonumber to a C bool); an error raised as a tail call has no Lua frame. Approved departure A33-depctrl-linux (2026-10-03): the legacy Linux package lacked DependencyControl's modules, so it failed to load there; the rewrite builds them on both platforms. Approved A33-depctrl-toolbox (2026-10-04): the DependencyControl Toolbox loads although the legacy capture reports it failing. Compared 2026-10-04 with the [legacy Windows capture](../../tools/legacy-capture/captures/windows-20d647c4/observations.json) (release v0.0.1-rc.1, zip sha256 20a1b0fa…9a1e; load-time and macro-time probe records): all twelve bundled scripts load in both with the same script_* values and registrations; legacy Windows also loads the DependencyControl Toolbox (A33-depctrl-linux concerns Linux only). `compare_automation.py` candidates against the rewrite on Ubuntu and on Windows (identical) were: (1) `aegisub.parse_karaoke_data` and `aegisub.get_frequency_peaks` exist during a legacy macro run (`AutomationToFile.cpp:1190-1191`) and not in the rewrite: now added as legacy AutoToFile does, for each run beside set_undo_point and kept after it (`lua_helper_tests` KaraokeAndPeaksExistFromTheFirstRun, ParseKaraokeDataFollowsTheLegacyParser, GetFrequencyPeaksKeepsTheLegacyChecks). parse_karaoke_data keeps LuaParseKaraokeData's quirks: the syllables go into the line table passed in (index 0 an empty one) and that table is returned; a syllable's text is wxString arithmetic over ParseTags positions ("{}" dropped, a part without '{' cut at a '\\' position taken from the part); no karaoke tag gives the whole line as one "k" syllable; the line's text is TextTl when set. get_frequency_peaks keeps its checks and messages in legacy's order ("Non number argument…", "needs loaded audio by FFMS2" without an audio box, "cannot get audio provider" while the box has no audio yet, "start or end time less than zero", {} when start ≥ end, the peek clamped to 0-1000, intensities only with peek 0) and reads the audio box's audio through a new FrequencyPeaks host service over a port of AudioSpectrum::CreateRange and the GFFT float FFT (`audio_spectrum.cpp`; `automation_services_tests` FrequencyPeaks.*, RouterTest.FrequencyPeaksReadTheAudioBox) with legacy's integer arithmetic (`unsigned long` per platform). Not reproduced: legacy reads the video's FFMS2 provider before the audio box, so with an FFMS2 video whose audio it had not loaded it read uninitialised samples (and with another file in the box, the video's audio); the rewrite reads the box only and raises the no-audio error then (proposed under R3-hang-crash-loss, not decided); a sample rate below 2048 (legacy divided by zero) gives no peaks; legacy's per-CPU spectrum cache threads are one line at a time on the GUI thread. (2) `require "l0.DependencyControl"` from the probe's top level during load failed in the rewrite (`…:279: attempt to index a nil value`) and succeeded in legacy: not a host difference. DependencyControl's first require in a fresh configuration runs its update check, which fetches live third-party feeds; with them reachable its own UpdateFeed template expansion fails on the current Aegisub-Motion feed (reproduced: online it fails, under `unshare -n` it loads); a later require skips the check (lastUpdateCheck, written before the check). The legacy capture's check evidently reached no feed. The helper tests now keep DependencyControl's updater off (`updaterEnabled` false in its config) so the corpus no longer depends on the network, and `DependencyControlLoadsWhileTheScriptLoads` pins that the load-time environment (decode_path answered during the load, include path, native modules) is enough for it. Compared again 2026-10-04 on Ubuntu ([compare-rewrite-ubuntu.txt](../../tools/legacy-capture/captures/windows-20d647c4/compare-rewrite-ubuntu.txt)): the only corpus difference left is (3) host `jit.version`, recorded as an observed difference, not changed: "LuaJIT 2.1.ROLLING" in the legacy Windows release vs the rewrite's "LuaJIT 2.1.<timestamp>" ("LuaJIT 2.1.1788856981"). Third-party corpus (2026-10-04, [manifest](../../tests/fixtures/automation-thirdparty/manifest.json): 41 scripts from 8 projects, each pinned to a commit with its licence and every file's sha256): unanimated (Unlicense), lyger, arch1t3cht, ILL, Myaamori, line0 (MIT) and PhosCity (GPL-3.0) are committed under `tests/fixtures/automation-thirdparty/<project>@<sha>/` with their licence; petzku (no licence file) is fetched at test time from the pinned commit's raw URLs and checked against the sha256s (`automation-thirdparty-stage`, `stage.cmake`; offline its scripts are skipped with the reason, `FetchedProjectsAreAvailable` skips). Lua and MoonScript, with and without DependencyControl (required, and optional as in the ua.* scripts), karaskel, FFI/requireffi (ILL), re/unicode, clipboard, dialogs, validation functions; no third-party script registers a filter. Staged as a user installs them (Autoload, the bundled Include plus the projects' modules; bundled modules are not replaced). `hikari_backends_automation_thirdparty_tests` pins against [expected.json](../../tests/fixtures/automation-thirdparty/expected.json) (artifacts `automation-thirdparty-corpus.json`, `automation-thirdparty-runs.json`): on Ubuntu, with the Lua 5.2 extensions (L6-lua52-linux) and SubInspector built for Linux (A33-subinspector-linux, both 2026-10-05), 35 of 41 load and register (ua.* 7, petzku 7, lyger GradientByChar/LayerIncrement/FbfTransform/LuaInterpret/GradientEverything, split-tags, arch CenterTimes/FocusLines/RWTools/NoteBrowser/Line2Fbf/SplitSections, l0 CascadeTransforms/Nudge/SplitLines/VerticalText, phos snap/wave/FoldOperations/EditTags, myaa.PasteFromPad); 6 fail to load: arch.ConvertFolds and myaa.Bounce on the bundled MoonScript's parser ("Failed to parse"), the two ILL scripts and phos.RemoveTags (through phos.AssfPlus, which requires ILL) on the absent clipper2 native library (ILL ships it through its DependencyControl feed), and myaa.MergeScripts on its missing module myaa.pl (Myaamori's Penlight, not in the corpus). DependencyControl tries to install a missing module from its feed while the script loads even with the updater off, so MergeScripts' reason depends on the network and only its load result is pinned (`loaded_only`); lyger.FbfTransform and GradientEverything register one macro per preset in pairs() order, which LuaJIT does not fix between processes, so their macros are pinned by name (`macros_unordered`). Before L6-lua52-linux 23 loaded (Functional's table.pack failed 13, MoonScript's `continue` phos.EditTags); between it and A33-subinspector-linux 27 (SubInspector's absent library failed the 9 over ASSFoundation). SubInspector: the legacy package shipped the v0.5.1 DLL (`si_getVersion` 0x000501) for Windows only; [Thirdparty/SubInspector](../../Thirdparty/SubInspector/README.md) holds that release's unchanged sources (TypesettingTools/SubInspector 931377c4, MIT, COPYING), built on Linux as `libSubInspector.so` against the rewrite's libass with `NO_ZLIB` as legacy, exporting only si_*, and staged as Include/SubInspector/Inspector/libSubInspector.so; Windows keeps the legacy DLL. `lua_helper_tests` SubInspectorMeasuresARenderedLine renders a line through it. v0.5.1's own libass had its rasterizer approximation patched out; the rewrite's libass 0.17.5 is unpatched, so bounds can differ from the DLL's by that precision (not observed). Seven macro runs on [fixture.ass](../../tests/fixtures/automation-thirdparty/fixture.ass) through the host ([runs.json](../../tests/fixtures/automation-thirdparty/runs.json): snapshot, run, dialogs answered through the dialog handler, apply as one undo step, undo restores): ua Cycles/Blur Cycle, ChangeCase (dialog, UPPERCASE with ß→SS), MultiLineEditor Reverse (dialog), lyger LayerIncrement (dialog) each one step; petzku SmartQuotify (aegisub.re look-behinds, typographic quotes) and lyger GradientByChar (bundled LibLyger, which iterates the subtitles object with ipairs through its __ipairs) each one step; ua JumpToNext/Style returns a selection and adds no step. Before L6-lua52-linux SmartQuotify and GradientByChar failed on `ipairs(subs)`. Windows is observed and pinned (2026-10-14): the Winix VM run (job-a6dfbb66) differed from the Linux pins only in the three clipper2 load errors, whose Windows form (clipper2.dll, the Windows loader's message, DependencyControl's logger turning the drive letter into "C: /" and requireffi doubling separators) is pinned as `load_windows`, and in lyger.FbfTransform and GradientEverything, already pinned by name (`macros_unordered`); Windows paths read as `<stage>` (`ThirdPartyMessages` WindowsPathsReadAsStagePaths, LinuxPathsReadAsStagePaths). Windows CI 38542817278 at b4634e9e9 passes every `ThirdPartyCorpus` test against these pins in both jobs. Legacy comparison: the probe takes the same corpus and runs in both hosts (`drive.py --corpus-dir`, the probe's runs answer dialogs themselves, so legacy does not deadlock); [Linux capture](../../tools/legacy-capture/captures/linux-thirdparty-20d647c4/observations.json) (hikari-legacy-env, package sha256 e8d901b3…c0ed) and [comparison](../../tools/legacy-capture/captures/linux-thirdparty-20d647c4/compare-rewrite-ubuntu.txt) with `ThirdPartyCorpus.CaptureProbeRunsTheCorpus`: legacy Linux loads 17, every one of which also loads in the rewrite with the same registrations except as DependencyControl changes them; the rewrite loads six more (lyger ×3, petzku Accenter/JumpToNextPlus/Typewriter) and its ua.* and phos scripts take their DependencyControl path (versions "2.0.0" for "2.0", phos menus without the "PhosCity/" prefix), all A33-depctrl-linux; every failure both share has the same cause; the four runs both hosts completed (Cycles, JumpToNext, ChangeCase and MultiLineEditor Reverse, two of them dialog macros answered by the probe) leave identical events and return identical selections. Compared again after L6-lua52-linux (2026-10-05): `lua_features` now differ as approved (legacy Linux: no table.pack, no __ipairs, no break anywhere, string.buffer present; the rewrite: the legacy Windows build's), the rewrite loads ten more scripts than legacy Linux, and SmartQuotify, which fails in legacy Linux on ipairs(subs), runs in the rewrite; since legacy cannot show a script dialog (A33-dialog-deadlock), the host dialog path of the dialog macros is compared by source only. Fix: the legacy Windows build compiles LuaJIT with LUAJIT_ENABLE_LUA52COMPAT and LUAJIT_DISABLE_BUFFER (`Thirdparty/Build/LuaJit/LuaJit.vcxproj`); the rewrite's LuaJIT (ports/luajit) now does on Windows (R5-per-platform) and, by the approved L6-lua52-linux (2026-10-05), on Linux too, so table.pack, __ipairs on the subtitles object (LibLyger, SmartQuotify) and MoonScript's `continue` work on both. `lua_helper_tests` LuaJitBuildMatchesTheLegacyPlatform pins the build (the same on both platforms); on Windows it passes in CI (38542817278 at b4634e9e9). Candidates: (a) the Lua 5.2 extensions on Linux: approved and done (L6-lua52-linux); (b) SubInspector's native library on Linux: approved and done (A33-subinspector-linux); (c) legacy's ASS load adds "YCbCr Matrix: TV.601" to Script Info when it is missing or None (`SubsLoader.cpp:167-168`), so a macro's subtitles object has that info line in legacy and not in the rewrite: covered by C03-ycbcr-on-load (2026-10-05, Script Info stays as loaded), so the rewrite's behaviour is the approved one and the probe comparison's header difference is expected; (d) the bundled MoonScript cannot parse arch.ConvertFolds and myaa.Bounce in either host. Windows CI passes the third-party corpus, LuaJitBuildMatchesTheLegacyPlatform and SubInspectorMeasuresARenderedLine (the legacy DLL). Not yet: a legacy third-party capture on Windows (drive_windows.py has no corpus mode; the third-party scripts are compared with the legacy Linux capture, the bundled ones also with the legacy Windows release); packaging the staged modules (Phase 5) |
| [H2](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/125) | Real-device audio on Windows hardware: listening for I3, device handling for N6 and general playback; decision 60 (2026-10-16, "Follow + device picker"): both audio paths follow the system's default output device, reopen on a default change or the loss of the device in use mid-playback without an error, and Settings > Audio > Audio output > Device chooses a device (approved departure H2-output-device) | Windows half, 2026-10-15 ([results](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/blob/evidence/win-hw-20261015/evidence/win-hw-20261015/h2/results.md), host RUBY, silent-volume fixtures at −24 dBFS): audition content A2–A8 as specified; findings A9/B6 (no following during playback), A10 (a new audition stayed on the old default), A11 (WASAPI unplug: "Audio player failed."; DirectSound moved by Windows), B3 and B7 (Qt Multimedia on the old device; silent after an unplug, 165 `AUDCLNT_E_DEVICE_INVALIDATED`). The fix, with fakes for the device list and the output: `audio_devices_tests` (9: route resolution, default change, removal of the device in use, a chosen device missing at start, kept on a default change and returning, the picker's entries, unregistering), `audio_box_player_tests` (+6: a default change idle reopens after a rescan at the next play; playing, it reopens at once and the new device's first sample is the frame being heard; a lost device reopens on the default from the frames taken, with no failure; with no device it waits on its frame and goes on at the next change; after the retries it fails with "Audio player failed."; the chosen device by name in the default's host API, else the default; the fill thread's recovery through the owner), `options_dialog_tests` TheOutputDeviceIsChosenByIdWithItsName (the setting persisted by id with its name, a missing choice kept, System default stores nothing), `general_player_tests` outputDeviceFollowsTheRequest (a real QAudioOutput takes each listed device and the default; skips where the host has none), `shell_tests` audioOutputDeviceFollowsTheSystemAndTheChoice (the picker lists System default and the devices, the choice persists across runs, both paths move on a default change and an unplug while the audio box plays, a missing choice falls back and returns) | The user waived a new listening session after the fix (decision 60): switching is observed through fakes and the CI runs below, not on real hardware. Linux: the editor output ignores a chosen device (PortAudio's ALSA list has no sink names) and plays through the sound server's default route; the Linux half of H2 (a real unplug through PipeWire) was not run. | [E3](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/141) | Translation operations in the Grid (GRID_PASTE_TRANSLATION "Paste translation text", GRID_TRANSLATION_DIALOG "Dialogue shifting window" / TLDialog "Translation matching options") | `grid_translation_tests` (13), against legacy SubsGrid::OnPasteTextTl, SetTlMode and MoveTextTL at 20d647c4: entries fill the shown Lines' translations in order, then appended Lines (zero times, the Default Style, no text); .srt blocks, .ass Dialogue lines, other files line by line, the file read through OpenWrite::FileOpen as `backends::readLegacyTextFile` reads it (R4-uchardet charset; R5-per-platform line ends: CRLF read as LF on Windows, "\r" kept on Linux, where a blank CRLF line is an empty entry); a file FileOpen cannot read, decode or finds empty changes nothing, as legacy returned, with the rewrite's "Could not open %1; nothing was changed." log line; translation mode turned on (TLmode Style copied from Default with alignment 8, "TLMode Style", "TLMode: Yes", "TLMode Showtl: Yes") and saved as legacy pairs, appended Lines included; one "Pasting translation" step whose Undo restores Lines, Script Info and Styles; the six MoveTextTL modes with the count from the first two selected rows, each one "Moving translation text" step. `ass_save_tests` (Script Info and Style edits keep every other byte). `shell_tests`: Paste translation text from a file and the window's Delete line (Translation) with Undo | Characterized legacy behaviour kept: an SRT file's last block is never pasted on Windows (CRLF files read as LF too); on Linux a blank CRLF line after it ends it, so it is pasted (R5-per-platform); rows past the count without a partner keep their translation. | [D1](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/152) | Docking: the Classic panels (Video, Audio, Line editor, Grid, Reference) dock, split, tab, float, close and reopen; View > Panels (Show/Hide/Float/Dock per panel), Reset layout, Restore the previous layout; layout persistence | `docking_qualification_tests` (6): the pinned engine (ports/kddockwidgets: KDDockWidgets 2.4.1 at c1d28d25, QtQuick only, static, against Qt 6.11.2) builds the default arrangement, floats and redocks, closes and reopens a panel keeping its guest, tabs, round-trips LayoutSaver and refuses corrupt or newer payloads keeping the live layout; also on Windows (Winix job-6f6b593e). `workspace_layout_tests` (8, now 10): Hikari's envelope (schema, panel registry, engine version, preset, payload) with IDs validated before the engine; a floated and a closed panel come back next session; closing the main window saves the arrangement it showed, the last save of the session, so the docked panels and a floating one come back with the window shown (the engine closes every panel on the window's close, before the window's closing handler, and the Windows gate at 22a2a452 restarted to a hidden window after a save during the teardown), and a close the review holds back keeps every panel (`close_review_tests` cancellingTheReviewKeepsThePanels); Reset layout and the backup; corrupt, newer, foreign-engine and engine-refused layouts are kept as layout.json.unrestored, named in a notice, and the Editing layout shown. `shell_tests`: View > Panels Float moves the Line editor into its own window with the draft and the editing target kept, Dock returns it (F6 into the floating panel and Ctrl+Shift+H from it are a separate test that needs the platform to activate the floating window; Xvfb without a window manager does not, and the test says so), Hide/Show keep the Grid's content and focus it; View > Move panel… (the keyboard placement window) puts Audio left of the Grid, resizes it to a typed width and tabs it with the Grid, keeping the focus on the moved panel. `docking_qualification_tests` also moves and tabs an already-docked panel ; the D1 gate fixes add, failing before and passing after: Float, F6/Shift+F6 and Show activate the floating panel's window and the main one by the application's focus window (the main window takes the Grid when it activates with nothing focused), View > Move panel… shows its defaults and keeps keyboard changes, the file drop target refuses non-file drags and is not what the engine's hit test finds, title-bar buttons and tabs (each with Float and Close) are named buttons and page tabs (D3 replaced the buttons with the "⋯" menu), the dock title bar is each panel's only header (2026-10-05; the panels keep their accessible names, which `shell_tests` checks in place of the removed `<panel>Title` labels) and the dock chrome (title bars, panel tabs, separators) takes the application palette's colours, the Grid's table is in the accessibility tree under a panel named Grid, a floating panel off every screen comes back (also after restoring a layout), and the spelling notice uses native separators | The Classic arrangement's sizes (2026-10-04, with the A2-A4 integration): the top row asks for 520 px and the Audio dock for 272, so at the default 1280 x 800 window the audio box has legacy's AUDIO_BOX_HEIGHT (170 px) above the Line editor (`shell_tests` audioBoxOpensAFileAndShowsItsWaveform). Open (card stays open). Linux native gate (tools/native-gate, `out/native-gate-evidence/` of the D1 fix; sway 1.12, KWin 6.7.5 `--virtual`, mutter 50.5 `--headless`, Xvfb 21.1 + openbox, Orca 50.3, Qt 6.11.2, KDDockWidgets 2.4.1). Observed on sway, KWin, mutter and X11: default arrangement, keyboard Float/Dock with the draft and target kept, Move panel… defaults and placement, focus after Float, Video stepping docked/floating/redocked, layout persistence, Orca speaking panel and menu names, Alt+V from the Line text, named title-bar buttons and tabs pressed through AT-SPI, the Grid table under the Grid panel. F6/Shift+F6 into and out of a floating panel, Ctrl+Shift+H from it, F6 with the main window fullscreen and Show after a monitor removal: observed on KWin, mutter and X11; on sway with its default `focus_on_window_activation urgent` the shell's xdg_activation_v1 request (a token from the focused surface with its key serial) only marks the window urgent (`sway/xdg-activation-trace.txt`), and with `focus_on_window_activation focus` all of them are observed (`sway-activate/`). Drag-to-dock with real pointer input and drop indicators: sway, KWin, X11 (on Wayland the engine's QtQuick frontend takes drag-and-drop drops only in floating windows; the shell gives the main window the same receiver). Mixed DPI: sway, KWin (2x rendering also reviewed on mutter). Monitor removal: sway, KWin, X11 (the shell moves the window back; Xvfb's output shrunk with its RandR monitor), mutter reviewed from screenshots. Fullscreen: sway, KWin, X11 (panel visible above the fullscreen main window). Not observable: on mutter without gnome-shell no client window geometry (double-click/drag on a floating title, monitor placement), no fullscreen request path; X11 has no per-monitor scale. The real-platform test run skips the activation-dependent test on mutter (synthetic QTest keys carry no input serial, so mutter refuses the token) and on default sway; window placement tests skip on Wayland. NVDA, Windows and its drag-and-drop are a separate Windows gate. Presets: View > Layout preset applies Editing, Timing (Video closed), Translation or Typesetting (Audio closed) as starting arrangements, records the preset in the envelope and Reset layout returns to it (`workspace_layout_tests`); the tools these presets also open (Timing, History, Search, Styles) arrive with their tool cards. Reordering tabs inside a group from the keyboard (Move before/after): the "⋯" menu's Move tab left and Move tab right (`shell_tests` panelMenuMovesTabsWithinTheirGroup: off at the bar's end, the moved panel stays shown with the focus, the order survives a layout restore, a lone panel has neither item). Not yet: layouts per Workspace (one shared layout for now). The Classic shortcuts (F4, F5, Shift+Del, F6 and the menus' Open, Close, Save, Save as, History) work while a floating panel has the focus and not in dialogs or tool windows; the shell compares the application's focus window, because Qt also reports the main window active while its floating child has the focus. Saving runs on a 5 s check of the arrangement and at close, not on each engine event. Layout persistence in the Linux gate (branch w5-misc-gaps, 2026-10-13): `gate.py persist` now checks every shown panel, not only Audio: its window (docked in the main window or floating), its rectangle in that window, the floating windows' and the main window's sizes (within 2 px) and, on X11, the floating windows' positions (Wayland compositors place windows themselves), each reading taken once two readings 0.5 s apart agree, a size it cannot read left uncompared, and re-reading once after 4 s before it fails. Run on this branch's build (out/native-gate-evidence-persist, not kept in the repository): X11 observed (Audio floating, Video and Grid docked, all in place); sway, KWin and mutter failed: the floating Audio window comes back at its size (797x229 on sway) but the Audio panel inside it is 35 px shorter (sway 779x176 before, 779x141 after; KWin and mutter 621x125, then 621x90), leaving an empty band at the bottom of the window; the docked panels and the main window are unchanged. The gate's reading was ruled out as the cause (2026-10-13, from the persist-0, -1 and -2 evidence): the floating window's size and the panel's origin inside it (9,44: the 8 px shadow, the frame line and the 35 px header) are the same before and after, only the panel's height drops, by exactly the header's 35 px, and the screenshots show the panel's "No audio open" moved up by half that; X11, where the panel starts at 1,36 without the drawn shadow, keeps its size. So the app restores the floating panel 35 px short on Wayland. Fixed on branch w5-d1float (2026-10-13): the engine rebuilt the floating window's layout under the window's own title bar, hid that title bar once the layout had its lone group, and ignored the layout area's resize while the restore ran; on X11 the window's restored size reached its contents after the restore and refitted the layout, on Wayland nothing followed. The shell now fits every floating window's layout to its area after each restore (`fitFloatingLayouts`, docs/qt/docking.md), and `workspace_layout_tests` aFloatingPanelComesBackFillingItsWindow checks it (offscreen, with the drawn shadow: without the fix the restored layout is 782x747 in an area of 782x782). Native gate `persist` at the same source, without and with the fix (out/native-gate-evidence-before and -after, not kept in the repository): sway 779x176 before exit, 779x141 after restart without the fix, 779x176 with it; KWin and mutter 621x125, then 621x90 without and 621x125 with; X11 637x141 both ways; the floating windows (797x229 sway, 639x178 the others) and the docked panels unchanged; layout-persistence observed on all four sessions with the fix. D1's other gates still run at the merged head. |

the other 3 are F1-stale-replace, F1-end-of-text and F1-empty-match, whose legacy premises the captures confirm (the stale replace, two hangs). The captured plain search folds A-Z only with the English interface (legacy never sets the C locale; wxGTK disables GTK's setlocale): approved departure U1-unicode-case: the rewrite's Find and replace folds every letter by Unicode simple case folding (QString::toCaseFolded, as ripgrep and JavaScript's Unicode regular expressions do) whatever the interface language, `shell_tests` caseFoldingIgnoresCaseForEveryLetter; a regular expression still folds every letter. Legacy Windows is expected to match (the release ships with an English interface and never calls setlocale, so the UCRT runs in the "C" locale, where towlower/towupper change A-Z only); not verified on the VM: Microsoft's documentation only says the conversion is locale-specific, and UCRT iswupper may still classify Latin-1 capitals (U+00C0-U+00DE) as upper there. | R1-pcre2: regular expressions run through core::LegacyRegex as wxRegEx(find, wxRE_ADVANCED, plus wxRE_ICASE without "Match case"): PCRE2 10.47 with UTF and DOTALL ("." matches the translation separator, lookbehind and Unicode case folding as legacy); a PCRE2 match error (match or heap limit) is logged "Failed to find match for regular expression: ..." and counts as no match, as wx_regexec does (std::regex could throw on Windows). Approved departures (R3-hang-crash-loss): F1-empty-match (an empty regular-expression match in Replace all or Replace checked is replaced once and the search moves on one character; legacy wxRegEx::Replace repeated it forever); F1-end-of-text (Find all with a plain "End of text" records a Line once; legacy recorded it forever); F1-link-loop (a folder inside itself through a link, a real cycle, is cut; legacy recursed until the path was too long, listing and replacing the same files dozens of times from several threads at once; a folder reached twice without a cycle is listed twice, as legacy); F1-file-lines (replacing in files writes skipped Comment lines, blank lines and every unchanged line as they were read; legacy dropped the first two and trimmed the rest); F1-checked-file-lines (Replace checked in files keeps a Line edited on disk, logged, and every later Line; legacy left them all out of the file); F1-checked-step (Replace checked records one "Replace all" step per changed Document; legacy recorded each Document's step on the previous tab when the next started, so the last Document's change could get none); F1-stale-replace (Replace next does not replace a match it already replaced, or whose Line changed since, when its own search found nothing; legacy overwrote [start, end) of whatever the Line held then, e.g. "c dog c x" became "c dog c"; a match still there is replaced as legacy did); F1-out-of-range (a stale Replace next position past the text, legacy std::out_of_range, and Replace checked offsets outside the Line are bounds-checked and replace nothing); F1-file-write (a failed or short write puts the file's original bytes back, logged; legacy left a truncated file; its replacements still count, as legacy's did); F1-file-untouched (legacy never reset SubsAllReplacements between the files of a thread's slice, so after the slice's first replacement every later file that reached the write, matching or not, was copied to ReplaceBackup and rewritten as UTF-8 with a BOM, CRLF, trimmed lines and without its blank and skipped Comment lines, and an unreadable non-.ass file was truncated to a BOM; only files with a replacement are written, and the overcounted total is shown as legacy showed it); F1-file-header-cr (on Linux legacy wrote each CR LF header line of an .ass file as CR CR LF; each keeps one CR LF); F1-srt-leading-lines (legacy read an SRT cue's Start from the start of the cue, so lines before its " --> " line went into the hours and were lost: on Linux every CRLF file is one cue starting with "1\r", whose Start moved by an hour; the times come from the " --> " line, a leading cue number gives way to the count legacy writes in its place, other leading lines stay); F1-file-ctrlz (legacy's Windows text-mode read stopped at a Ctrl+Z byte, 0x1A, which UTF-16 text such as U+011A Ě contains, and a replacement then wrote the file without the rest; the whole file is read); F1-utf16-crlf (in UTF-16 and UTF-32 the bytes 0D 0A can be one character, U+0D0A in UTF-16BE, or straddle two, U+0D15 U+0D0A is 15 0D 0A 0D in UTF-16LE; legacy Windows' text-mode read deleted that 0D and shifted every later byte, so the rest of the file was garbled and a replacement wrote it back so; these charsets are never folded); F1-sjis-backslash (extended R3: glibc's SHIFT_JIS reads the ASCII bytes 0x5C and 0x7E as U+00A5 and U+203E, so legacy Linux read "{\an8}" as "{¥an8}" and a file replace wrote every tag back with yen signs; on Linux '\' and '~' are kept for any charset whose iconv maps those single bytes elsewhere, of uchardet's names only SHIFT_JIS, whose other sequences never give U+00A5 or U+203E; Windows' code page 932 kept them already). Approved separately, each with its own row: F1-win-charsets (above) and F1-utf16-bom: legacy Windows decoded uchardet's "UTF-16" with wx's native little-endian converter, keeping a little-endian BOM as U+FEFF, written back as a second BOM, and byte-swapping a big-endian file, so Find in files found nothing in it; legacy Linux read no UTF-16 or UTF-32 file with a BOM at all (wxString's conversion ran iconv twice on one handle, and the second pass read the BOM as U+FEFF and overflowed the counted buffer, so FileOpen read "" and the file was skipped); on both the BOM decides and is removed. S57-from-selected, characterized: From selected (and no Lines button) starts Find, Find all and Replace all at the first selected shown Line and includes it, and a wrap starts there again (unchanged); the misbehaving case, only hidden Lines selected, made legacy search from row 0, before the selection, and now starts at the first selected Line. Accepted design (reviewed-surface-layouts.md, Search B "Scope rail"): find and replace is the persistent Search tool (a dock that starts closed below the Grid) with the mode, query and scope controls on the left and the results with their checks and Replace on the right; Ctrl+F, Ctrl+H, F3 and the Edit menu stay, and the same shortcut while the tool has the focus on that tab hides it (legacy ShowDialog). The legacy message boxes are non-blocking dialogs shown one at a time; the search resumes with the answer and every other find call (F3, buttons, Replace checked) is refused meanwhile, as legacy's modal boxes blocked the window. As legacy: changing the editing target resets the search (OnPageChanged's FR->Reset()); the editor's selection becomes the search text whenever the tool gains the focus from elsewhere, not after a message box (blockTextChange), compared with the last match where the editor shows it (hidden tags move offsets); a file result goes to the Document that has the file, else opens as a new Document when the editing target has a file (InsertTab + OpenFile), else loads into the Untitled editing target (OpenFile), which asks to save its changes first through the close review (P2's reviewOpen); after Cancel, or when the open fails, the result's Line is taken from the Untitled Document (or the new empty tab) as legacy took it from the current tab; closing the review window is Cancel. Characterized legacy quirks kept: Replace all compares "^x" / "x$" literally when "Beginning/End of text" is used with regular expressions (nothing is replaced); a regular expression in Replace all continues after the pattern's length, not the match's; plain "Beginning of text" finds the first match anywhere in a Line; Find next stays on an empty regular-expression match; Replace next replaces the last match when its own search found nothing but the match is still there; Replace checked after a files search treats later Document results as files (nothing replaced); results in other fields never replace; "Search in subfolders" opens with the hidden-folders bit and "Search in hidden folders" unchecked; the Find in subtitles tab never clears option bits from 512 up; Cancel in the style chooser empties the styles; backups of same-named files overwrite each other. Files (R4-uchardet, R5-per-platform; backends::readLegacyTextFile): links are followed as wxDir follows them (a link target is written through the link); a UTF-8 BOM or valid non-ASCII UTF-8 is UTF-8, otherwise uchardet names the charset (pure ASCII included) and wxCSConv's decoder reads it as each platform did: on Windows the code page wx maps the name to (fmapbase.cpp CharsetToEncoding, msw/utils.cpp wxEncodingToCodepage; UTF-8 as 65001, EUC-KR as 949, ISO-2022-JP as 50222) through MultiByteToWideChar with MB_ERR_INVALID_CHARS, so invalid bytes and code pages that refuse the flag fail, and a name wx does not know (UHC, ISO-8859-16, VISCII, TIS-620, IBM852, GB18030, ...) through ICU (F1-win-charsets: legacy read them as Latin-1, wxCSConv without a converter, and a file replace wrote the mojibake back; ICU lacks ISO-8859-16, VISCII and GEORGIAN-*, which read as nothing); when IsValidCodePage or GetCPInfo refuses the code page wx went on to its own wxEncodingConverter tables (ISO-8859-x, WINDOWS-125x, KOI8-R), for which Qt's decoder of the name stands in, else ICU (F1-win-charsets); on Linux glibc iconv as wxMBConv_iconv used it (to UTF-32, run twice on one handle as wxString's conversion did, any EILSEQ, EINVAL or E2BIG failing the file: UHC, ISO-8859-16, VISCII and GEORGIAN-* included), with a name iconv cannot open (HZ-GB-2312) read as Latin-1; ASCII and ISO-8859-1 as Latin-1 on both (wxCSConv converts neither); with no name the system code page; bytes the charset cannot decode, or an empty file, are read as nothing and the file is skipped (legacy FileOpen read ""); changed files are written UTF-8 with a BOM; the Windows build folds CRLF to LF before decoding (text mode; never in UTF-16/32, F1-utf16-crlf), the Linux build keeps every CR, with its consequences above; folders are listed as wxDir::Traverse lists them (application::legacy_dir): subfolders first (listed with no pattern), then the files matching each filter, each in the file system's order with no sorting; on Windows FindFirstFileW("<folder>\\<filter>") with wx's CheckFoundMatch (PathMatchSpecW on the long name: the system also matches 8.3 short names, which wx then drops), any case, the folder and hidden checks on the entry's own attributes (hidden or system entries only when asked); on Linux readdir with wxMatchWild (case sensitive, no character classes, "." names only when asked) and stat for folders; every non-folder entry (broken links, fifos) a file; the only writes besides the listed files are the legacy backups in ReplaceBackup beside hikari.ini (none without a settings file); the files are processed one after another in path order: legacy's threads each took a consecutive slice and their results were shown in thread order, which is the same order, and the count follows the slices over legacy's processor count (findreplace.cpp numOfProcessors: GetSystemInfo's dwNumberOfProcessors, 4 when it is 0, on Windows; on Linux the build's GetSystemInfo, sysconf(_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN), the online processors whatever the affinity mask). Question texts and log lines are translated (tr() with the legacy English text as the source: "Invalid regular expression '%s': %s", "Failed to find match for regular expression: %s", "Line %i cannot be replaced,\ncause it was edited.", and the rewrite's "Cannot back up %s."); the Replace-outside-its-tab line, which legacy logged as an untranslated Polish debug text, stays English. Not yet: Actor/Effect matches select no field text (the Line editor has no Actor or Effect field yet); legacy captures on Windows and the CI run of the ui route (the reviewed capture is the local reproduction), legacy captures of Find/Replace in all open subtitles and the files tab; Windows (CI/Winix) runs, including the Win32 code-page decoding (the name table, strict 65001, the IsValidCodePage fallback, GetSystemInfo's count), the Windows listing (legacy_dir's FindFirstFileW lister and its Windows-only tests). Settings (O1 registry): FIND_REPLACE_OPTIONS and FIND_REPLACE_STYLES are find.options and find.styles, read when a tab shows (the tool's first opening, SetValues) and written by SaveValues when the tool switches tab, closes or runs, and when the application ends (HikariSubFrame::OnClose's FR->SaveOptions()); the four recent tables are find.recentFinds, find.recentReplacements, findInSubs.recentFilters and findInSubs.recentPaths, read when the tool is first opened (FindReplace's constructor, cut to 20) and written at each AddRecent as its SetTable calls write them (the finds; the replacements except on the Find tab; the filters and paths on the Find in subtitles tab). Set default and DestroyDialogs follow the O1 per-owner rule (see O1) |

Legacy Windows is expected to match (the release ships with an English interface and never calls setlocale, so the UCRT runs in the "C" locale, where towlower/towupper change A-Z only); not verified on the VM: Microsoft's documentation only says the conversion is locale-specific, and UCRT iswupper may still classify Latin-1 capitals (U+00C0-U+00DE) as upper there. | Rules compile with core::LegacyRegex, wxRE_ADVANCED and wxRE_ICASE without Match case (approved R1-pcre2); a PCRE2 match error (the match or heap limit) is logged "Failed to find match for regular expression: %s" as wx_regexec logs it and counts as no match: in Find and Replace all once for each failed Matches, which ends that rule's walk over the Line (a Line worked out again for the "Fixing minor errors" step is reported once), in the results' Replace before the find's "Cannot replace ..." line. Rules.txt is read through backends::readLegacyTextFile, the reader F1 uses (R4-uchardet: UTF-8 with its BOM or as IsUTF8withoutBOM accepts it, else uchardet's charset decoded as wxCSConv did on each platform, glibc iconv on Linux and the code page wx maps the name to on Windows, names wx cannot map decoded by ICU on Windows (F1-win-charsets), else wxConvLocal; a failed conversion reads as nothing and gives the shipped rules; F1's row has the details and that reader's departures, which apply here too: F1-file-ctrlz, F1-utf16-crlf, F1-sjis-backslash and the approved F1-utf16-bom) and written back as legacy wrote it (UTF-8 with a BOM, CRLF). R5-per-platform: on Windows the text-mode read folds CRLF; on Linux each line keeps its "\r", and a blank CRLF line becomes an invalid "\r" rule that is logged. F4-rules-cr (extended R3-hang-crash-loss): legacy Linux split the checkbox line with its "\r", so the last token was "1\r", not "1", and the last rule came back unchecked after every restart; read by the Linux build, the checkbox line's trailing "\r" is dropped first (`readReplacerRules`' RulesReadBy::Linux), so every rule keeps its state; the rule lines are unchanged (their last field, the options, is read with wxAtoi, which stops at the "\r"). Applied conditional approvals (2026-09-29, applied as written; together with R3-hang-crash-loss). They rest on the legacy source and the round-1 fixtures, which reproduce both defects; the legacy capture S57 asks for (rules '(  +)'->' ' then 'XY'->'z' on 'a  b XY', and the From-selected cases) is for the integrator to gather: S57-rule-offsets — in Replace all each rule searches the text the previous rule produced, so all shipped rules on "Hello , ty sie  bede..ok" give "Hello, ty się będę...ok" as the results' Replace does (legacy began each later rule on the original text with its positions applied to the changed text, so only the first change landed); S57-from-selected — "From the selected line" takes the first selected shown Line and every later one, numbered by shown Lines, and every Line without a selection (legacy started its walk there and took no Line); the other three choices are unchanged. Approved departures (R3-hang-crash-loss): F4-empty-match — an expression that matches the empty string is replaced once per empty match and the search moves on by one character (core::LegacyRegex::replace; legacy wxRegEx::Replace repeated it forever), and a find or Replace all walk stops after a match at the end of the text (legacy searched "" past the end forever); F4-stale-position — positions are recomputed after each replacement (S57-rule-offsets), so a later rule's position can no longer lie past the end of the changed text (legacy threw std::out_of_range from wxString::replace); F4-rule-bounds — a results row whose rule number has no compiled expression or rule is bounds-checked, not replaced and logged as "Cannot replace ..." (legacy read past rxrules and rules); F4-from-selected — the data-loss case above; F4-rules-cr — above. Characterized legacy quirks kept: MoveCase capitalizes the replacement's first character when the find has one capital anywhere ("Hello,World" becomes "HellO, World") and runs even when nothing was replaced; Replace all looks back for a tag only to the end of the previous find; an invalid checked rule shifts the rule numbers of later finds, and the results' Replace then uses the expression at that number among all rules that compile with the numbered rule's replace phrase; hidden Lines are always skipped, "Ignore filtering in some actions" or not (legacy skipped hidden Lines always and with it also NonDialogue lines, which are not Lines here); word boundaries, \w, \d and \s see ASCII only, as PCRE2 without UCP ("sieć" becomes "sięć", "bedę" is not found); a malformed Rules.txt line keeps the fields it had and is logged, its options 0 where legacy read an uninitialized value; "Find error" and "Replace error" are disabled and the "+" beside the styles does nothing; Rules.txt is written only when the application ends, as legacy's dialog destructor did. Layout: the Multireplacer and Search results stay two modeless dialogs (closePolicy Escape only) placed once, as legacy placed them (R5-per-platform): the Multireplacer centred (CenterOnParent); the results at wxDefaultPosition, which on Windows is the DLGTEMPLATE position HikariDialog's wxTOPLEVEL_EX_DIALOG gives (34x22 dialog units from the main window's client origin, without DS_ABSALIGN: 68x44 pixels with the system font's 8x16 base units, below the menu bar), so in a 1280x800 window they reach the rules list as legacy's did; on Linux wxGTK left the place to the window manager, which one window cannot ask, so the results open at the window's top-left corner, clear of the rules list and buttons (near-legacy: any window-manager placement policy could give it). Each is moved by dragging its title (HikariDialog's HTCAPTION) and kept where it was left; the drag stops where the title would leave the window (its top inside, 48 pixels of the window in view), as the system kept legacy's native caption reachable; the accepted Search scope rail ([reviewed surface layouts](ux/reviewed-surface-layouts.md), B) needs the shared persistent Search surface that no wave-4 card builds, so it is deferred with F1, F2 and F3. The Grid's changed-Line mark (legacy State 1) is E6's ([#188](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/188)). Not yet: the hotkey and toolbar entry for GLOBAL_MISSPELLS_REPLACER; Windows evidence (the MSVC build, the Windows halves of the platform-conditional tests and the code-page table) and the S57 legacy capture |
