UserDic.udic and user-added dictionaries live in <settings dir>/Dictionary, and the missing-dictionary message names that folder, where R6 puts them. A legacy user's E/Dictionary/UserDic.udic is not read in place: the settings importer (#44) carries it over (migration map resources.csv, "Personal dictionary"). On Windows Hunspell gets the normalized long-path form, \\\\?\\UNC\\server\\share\\... for a UNC location (legacy passed a wxConvLocal narrow path; the long form opens any name). Packaging dictionaries remains a distribution item (R2-hunspell), so until dictionaries ship a user without any sees legacy's missing-dictionary message once and spelling turns off, as legacy does. Approved departures (R3-hang-crash-loss): AvailableDics reads past a shorter .aff list (undefined): the pairing stops at its end; Replace reads the window's error list past its end when every word of the last Line was skipped, and after a Replace that found nothing (undefined): bounds-checked, an empty or wordless Replace does nothing; AddWord and the dictionary load pass a word the dictionary's encoding cannot hold to Hunspell as a null buffer (undefined, a crash): such a word is not added to the loaded dictionary (it is still written to UserDic.udic). Kept legacy behaviour: the spell checker starts once (SpellChecker::Get), so turning spelling on after it started off checks nothing until a language is chosen again; without dictionaries spelling turns itself off (SPELLCHECKER_ON, editor.spellchecker) with legacy's message; Replace skips the next word when it starts at the replaced word's offset, also on another Line; Replace all in translation mode writes an untranslated Line's corrected text into its translation, and the window checks the (empty) translation there; Ignore All matches any case; AvailableDics pairs by position, so a .dic without its .aff hides the dictionaries after it; ReplaceMisspell replaces a word with two characters or less of tags as plain text and a shorter replacement drops the letters past it; AddWord appends "\n" + word after RemoveWords' CRLF lines (the word alone for a missing or empty file); the removal list shows number and blank lines; Grid marks include bracket errors with spelling off, the editor shows none; UserDic.udic falls back to ISO-8859-1 as wxConvAuto does; each platform's legacy reading and listing (R5-per-platform): UserDic.udic in Windows text mode on Windows (CR LF folded, Ctrl+Z ends the file) and byte for byte on Linux, the Dictionary folder in the file system's own order (application::legacy_dir, shared with F1's find in files and the Options dialog's list: FindFirstFileW with wx's long-name check on Windows, readdir with wxMatchWild on Linux) without hidden entries; the walk visits hidden Lines and makes them active. Draft policy: the editor's draft commits when the Spellchecker window opens, regains focus or acts (the accepted commit-on-leave draft policy, an "Edit Line" step), where legacy left unsent typing to be overwritten; a word the commit (or anything else) made stale is not acted on: the action starts the walk again from the active Line and shows the new word, and a press on an action (the five action buttons or a suggestion, which takes focus on a click as any click on legacy's window activates it; ClickFocus only, so Tab still passes over the list as over legacy's one list control) that brings the window back does not swap the word or the suggestion list under the click. An invalid draft that the Block policy keeps stops the walk with "The edited line cannot be committed.". Other differences: no spelling backend (tests only) means no spell checker at all, without a message or a SPELLCHECKER_ON change; marks on hidden tags are not shown while tags are hidden; the Grid shows no marks in translation mode because legacy draws them only on the translation column, which is not built yet. Colours are legacy's EDITOR_SPELLCHECKER/GRID_SPELLCHECKER defaults. The options are O1 registry settings (editor.spellchecker, editor.dictionaryLanguage, editor.suggestionsOnDoubleClick, legacy SPELLCHECKER_ON, DICTIONARY_LANGUAGE, EDITBOX_SUGGESTIONS_ON_DOUBLE_CLICK) that the Options dialog's Editor page shows: OK/Apply act as legacy SetOptions (a changed SPELLCHECKER_ON is ClearErrs(true, value), so the Line editor's switch follows; a changed DICTIONARY_LANGUAGE is SpellChecker::Destroy and ClearErrs; the double-click option is read at the double click). The Grid and the menus read SPELLCHECKER_ON; the Line editor keeps its own switch (TextEditor::SpellCheckerOnOff), which the menu and the Options dialog set, so Set default turns the option on for the Grid and the menus but not for the editor, and does not restart the spell checker (`shell_tests` spellingAndAudioOptionsComeFromTheRegistry). Where SpellChecker::Get turns the option off (no dictionary, a failed start), the editor's switch stays on and, with no dictionary, marks brackets only, as legacy's TextEditor does. Not yet: Arabic and Hebrew dictionaries (legacy's BIDI reverse conversion before checking), marks on the translation column in translation mode and on swapped tags (GRID_HIDE_TAGS), the editor menu's web search entries, Show status bar and Automatically change quotes, packaged dictionaries, legacy captures of the segmentation on Windows (UTF-16 offsets) and the context menu's request position in a real window (offscreen, Qt reports the caret's position). Windows, the inline marks included (`shell_tests` offscreen): Windows CI [run 37240819196](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/actions/runs/37240819196) and Winix VM job-2866cbab23eb493883406c28656eee4a on 80f3fe44 (859 tests, 0 failures) |

every page lists its legacy controls in the legacy order with the legacy check box labels and nothing else; an index past its list writes -1, an update-check date written while the dialog is open survives OK, Set default shows the legacy refresh defects and OK writes them, the shift times panel keeps its values through Set default; the conversion catalog warns when missing, a chosen catalog lists its Styles, OK writes the catalog and the empty style; with the English program language a catalog differing only in a non-ASCII letter's case is missing and an ASCII case difference is found, with PROGRAM_LANGUAGE "pl" at startup both are found; the dictionaries come from F3's availableDictionaries over the settings folder's Dictionary, pairing the i-th .dic with the i-th .aff in the platform's listing order (on Linux checked against a readdir pairing); Set default keeps the recent video and audio lists, resets the keyframes list, gives the defaults to a Select dialog not opened yet and to a colour picker not created yet, keeps an opened Select dialog's options (written at its close) and recent searches (written at its next selection), a created picker's recent colours (until it is opened for another tab), the built tag buttons (pressing one reads its reset option, the count dialog starts from the stored 0 and adds buttons from their options), the Grid's hidden columns and "ignore filtering" while the filter takes its defaults and each toggle writes only its option; a changed program font saves and drops the Select dialog (DestroyDialogs) | Kept legacy behaviour (characterized): OK/Apply write each bound control whose value differs from the stored one, so the shown fallbacks are written (an unset tab-name limit becomes 40, an out-of-range zoom 200, a missing language "en", a missing catalog the current one, a missing style the first); a stored index past its choice (Autoload loading method, FFMS2 seeking, inactive lines) shows nothing and OK writes -1, a negative one is kept; two dictionaries with the same shown name select the first, and OK rewrites the option to its symbol; the zoom field is not an ID_NUMBER_CONTROL, so OK stores its digits as typed without the 100-1100 clamp; Set default resets every option immediately (Cancel does not undo it) and then refreshes the controls with ResetDefault's defects: the three index choices show nothing (FindString of "0"/"2"/"1") and OK writes -1, the language, dictionary, catalog and style choices show the entry at wxAtoi of their text (the first, or the previous selection when that index is past the list), number fields show the clamped default without the 40 fallback (an unset tab-name limit shows 20 and OK writes 20), the zoom shows its raw empty text; choosing a catalog loads it (the current catalog is saved first) and moves the style to the first entry, or keeps the old index when the catalog has no Styles so OK writes an empty style; the external fonts folder gets slashes for backslashes off Windows and a trailing separator, only when it changed, and the folder chooser returns native separators (wxDirDialog); the Subtitle properties labels pair with ASS_PROPERTIES_* as legacy pairs them ("Proofreading" EDITING, "Editing" UPDATE); the shift times panel reads SHIFT_TIMES_TIME (2000) and SHIFT_TIMES_OPTIONS (unset: backward) and keeps its values through Set default, writing them at its next change (legacy writes the panel back when it shifts or closes). Choices find entries as wxArrayString::Index(text, false) does: IsSameAs without case is CmpNoCase, the C runtime's _wcsicmp (Windows) or wcscasecmp (Linux) under the process locale, which legacy left "C" (ASCII letters only) unless a PROGRAM_LANGUAGE other than English initialized wxLocale at startup (every letter then folded; the rewrite folds every letter whatever the language, U1-unicode-case; the rewrite folds with Qt, locale-independent, and cannot see a Linux wxLocale::Init that failed for an ungenerated locale and left "C"); dictionaries are listed through F3's availableDictionaries in the order wxDir lists each folder on each platform (R5-per-platform, application::legacy_dir: FindFirstFileW with wx's long-name check on Windows, readdir with case-sensitive wxMatchWild on Linux; no sorting). Approved departures (R3-hang-crash-loss): AvailableDics reads aff[i] past its list when the Dictionary folder has fewer .aff than .dic files (an out-of-range read); the pairing stops at the shorter list. Set default (config::ResetDefault) resets the registry at once; state long-lived legacy objects hold survives and they write it back later: HikariSubFrame's recent subtitles, video and audio lists (SetRecent, OnClose) are kept at once; the keyframes list is not written at exit, so it resets (legacy writes its in-memory list at the next keyframes addition; nothing uses recent.keyframes at this base, so the integrator reproduces that with the keyframes recent list); SelectLines, once opened, keeps its options and recent searches (options back at its close, legacy at exit or DestroyDialogs; recent searches at its next selection), and a changed program font saves and drops it (DestroyDialogs; an open Select dialog is closed); DialogColorPicker, once created, keeps its recent colours until it is opened for another tab's Line editor (in legacy DialogColorPicker::Get also recreates it for another parent from the style editor's colour buttons, the automation dialog's colour buttons (ButtonColorPicker, ColorPicker.cpp:1248) and the Options colour list (OptionsDialog.cpp:937); the rewrite's Style manager, AutomationDialog (a Qt ColorDialog) and Options dialog do not route through this picker, so those paths never recreate it); EditBox keeps the tag buttons SetTagButtons built while pressing one reads its reset option (the rewrite has one Line editor for all tabs, so tabs opened after Set default show the built buttons too where legacy builds none, and the Manage menu keeps the buttons' names where legacy relists the options' names when the count grows); SubsGrid keeps its hidden columns and "ignore filtering" (per grid in legacy; one Grid here, so tabs opened later keep them too) while the filter options, copy/paste columns and the update checker read the defaults as legacy reads them when it acts; the shift times panel keeps its values; FindReplaceDialog, once created, keeps its controls (written back by SaveValues at its next tab change, run, closing or the application's end, taking the reset option's bits from 512 up on the Find in subtitles tab) and FindReplace its four recent lists (written at its next AddRecent, each list as SetTable writes it), while one never created reads the defaults when it first opens; MisspellReplacer has no options (Rules.txt); the Line editor keeps its spell-checking switch (TextEditor::SpellCheckerOnOff) and the spell checker its dictionary, while the Grid and the menus read the reset SPELLCHECKER_ON (`shell_tests` findAndMultireplacerFollowLegacyOwnersThroughSetDefault, spellingAndAudioOptionsComeFromTheRegistry). DestroyDialogs (a changed program font) runs as HikariSubFrame::DestroyDialogs: FR->SaveOptions() writes the Search tool's tab and the tool closes, its FindReplace (recent lists, results) dropped so the next opening reads the registry again; SL as above; MR->Destroy(): ~MisspellReplacer writes a non-empty Rules.txt, the Multireplacer closes and is built again (empty rule fields, "All lines", centred, rules read from Rules.txt) at its next opening. Approved departure (R3-hang-crash-loss), O1-destroyed-results: the find results and the Multireplacer's Search results close with their tool; legacy left FindReplaceResultsDialog and FindResultDialog (children of the main window) open over the deleted FindReplace and MisspellReplacer, so their next action was a use-after-free. Set default also runs Hkeys.ResetDefaults() and refreshes the hotkey list, and the "ignore filtering" hotkey toggles from the option (SubsGrid::OnAccelerator): both in the O2 row. Settings are written to the file when they change (one QSettings per store, synced by Qt), not only when legacy saves Config.txt. Live where the rewrite has the consumer: resolution warning, change active on selection, duplication, autosave count, conversion options, hidden columns, spelling (SetOptions: a changed SPELLCHECKER_ON is ClearErrs(true, value), a changed DICTIONARY_LANGUAGE is SpellChecker::Destroy and ClearErrs), and the audio box's cache, delay and stream options (read at its next open); the others are stored for their cards. Not yet: the one-shot Config.txt importer (wave 5 by the card, settings-import.md), Themes (excluded by the accepted settings decision), the Windows Associations page (left to its platform card), the subtitle display filter (VSFILTER_INSTANCE, renderer capability unresolved), translated languages (English only until catalogues ship), live font changes and external font reloading, the dictionary list from F3's checker (R6-dictionary-location: the Main page lists the settings folder's Dictionary, then the program folder's, a symbol once, through F3's availableDictionaries, as F3's checker reads them), CPS/wraps character counting and hide-original-on-video wiring, a font face the system substitutes (legacy writes the substitute), Windows evidence. The wave-4 interim keys are registry settings with their legacy names and defaults, read and written through the settings store (they never shipped, so nothing migrates them): FindReplace/\* are find.options, find.styles, find.recentFinds, find.recentReplacements, findInSubs.recentFilters and findInSubs.recentPaths; Spelling/\* are editor.spellchecker, editor.dictionaryLanguage and editor.suggestionsOnDoubleClick; Audio/RamCache, Audio/Delay, Audio/CacheFilesLimit, Video/AcceptedAudioStream and Recent/Audio are audio.ramCache, audio.delay, audio.cacheFilesLimit, video.acceptedAudioStream and recent.audio; the Multireplacer had none (its rules are Rules.txt) |
| [O2](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/174) | Shortcut editor (GLOBAL_SETTINGS' Hotkeys page, legacy OptionsDialog "Hotkeyss": "Choose filtering", the Function / Hotkey list with Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y, Map hotkey (and a double click), Restore default hotkey, Delete hotkey, OK/Apply, Set default's Hkeys.ResetDefaults()); the "Hotkey mapping" window (HkeysDialog) with its window choice and refusals; the conflict questions per window; the Shift+click gesture on menu items and mapped buttons (Hotkeys::OnMapHkey); the bindings of every GLOBAL_\*, GRID_\*, EDITBOX_\*, VIDEO_\* and AUDIO_\* action (Hotkeys.cpp IDS, HotkeysNaming, LoadDefault) stored as SaveHkeys lines in the registry, shared with the automation hotkeys (S2), and their routing in the Global, Subtitles, Editor and Video windows (HikariSubFrame/TabPanel::SetAccels) | `hotkeys_tests` (20), read against the legacy sources in the repository at 20d647c4: Hotkeys.h IDS (243 actions, equal to the migration map's actions.csv), HotkeysNaming (226 names), LoadDefault (86 defaults, 63 main and 23 audio), GetType, GetDefaultKey; SaveHkeys/LoadHkeys lines (partitioned by the Audio window; empty, script and sub-100 bindings not written; an unknown symbol is id 0, an unknown window letter -1, numbers read as ids); GetHKey's reading of accelerator text as Qt sequences; HkeysDialog::OnKeyPress (modifier order Alt-Ctrl-Shift-, Global/Editor keys 31-126 need a modifier, Ctrl alone with V/C/X/Z and Alt+F4 without Ctrl refused, FillTable names, '$'..'`' characters, other keys ignored); DialogueTextEditor's own keys; AddHotkeysOnList's order; the filter modes and selection; map, restore, delete, undo and redo with the static hotkeysCopy; the questions and their effects per window; mapping to another window; Set default; the gesture. `shell_tests`: hotkeysPageMapsAndOkInstalls (page order, filter, the mapping window, staged until OK, saved, installed, the new keys open History and the old do not, read back at the next start), hotkeysMappingRefusesAndAsks (the three refusals keep the window, Cancel/Switch/Set anyway, the window choice adds a row, restore, delete, undo/redo in the list, Escape, Cancel installs nothing, a later Apply writes the cancelled edits), hotkeysSetDefaultResetsInMemoryOnly, hotkeyGestureOnMappedButtonAndMenu (Shift+click on the Bold button and on the Grid menu's Duplicate lines, installed and saved at once, the new keys act there), hotkeysRouteByWindow, ignoreFilteringHotkeyTogglesFromTheOption, hotkeysShareTheAutomationBindings; review fixes (2026-10-04): `hotkeys_tests` GestureSwitchFollowsLegacyOrder, InvalidKeysAreReportedAsGetHKeyLogsThem, NumericLabelsAreReadWithEachPlatformsWxAtoi, FilteredListShowsAndHidesBlocks; `shell_tests` globalBindingsRouteEveryAction (the shell's table, an action of another window bound for the Global window, a Global id bound for the Subtitles window, a remapped Style manager, CheckLastKeyEvent, Ctrl+I in the start field is the Editor's), tabShortcutsFollowTheRegistry (Ctrl+T adds a tab; GLOBAL_NEXT_TAB remapped to Alt+PgDn switches tabs, Ctrl+PgDown no longer does), keypadKeysFireTheirBindings (keypad key events with KeypadModifier: "Num +", "Num ." (also a comma decimal key), "Num 5" in the Grid, "Num /" from the shell's table; the digit row and plain '+' do not), hotkeyGestureOnMenus (Subtitles > Style manager, the Grid's checkable Reverse filtering not switched), hotkeyGestureOnVideoButtonsAndFrameKeys (Stop's tooltip and Shift+click with the window choice; Left/Right step frames from the Grid; GLOBAL_NEXT_FRAME remapped), hotkeysListGutterShowsHiddenRows, invalidShortcutsAreLogged, audioBindingsForTheGlobalWindowAndCtrlClick, hotkeyGestureOnAMacro. `hikari_ui_shortcuts_workflow` (Spix, Linux and Windows): File > Settings > Hotkeys, Map hotkey on Close current tab, Ctrl+Shift+J, OK; Ctrl+W no longer closes the tab, Ctrl+Shift+J does | Kept legacy behaviour (characterized): the list runs over the names table from the highest id down, so Global first; bindings without a name (scripts, copies to another window) follow their window's names; GLOBAL_QUIT is never listed; once every binding is listed the remaining names are left out (a deleted tail binding, say Audio Commit alt, loses its row at the next start); two Subtitles rows read "Hide selected lines". A row changed under a filter stays shown until the next filtering; mapping for another window adds "<window> <name>" at the end (or changes that row) and selects nothing. Map hotkey compares with legacy's static hotkeysCopy, other ids only; the Video and Audio windows count as one, and a video play/seek id (2000-2003) as the same as any window but Global ("already exists for": Switch / Delete / Cancel), otherwise "already exists in another window / other windows" (Switch only with one, Delete, Set anyway, Cancel); Switch and Delete change the doubled window's bindings only, each through the row "<window> <names table name>", so a script's or a copied binding (no such row) keeps its keys and a shared name changes the first such row; Switch gives them the row's current keys; Restore default asks nothing; Undo writes each changed row's binding into the copy, and an undone added row's binding stays in it. hotkeysCopy is filled at the first edit while empty and never cleared: a later dialog's OK/Apply (with a change) writes a cancelled dialog's edits and reverts what the gesture, the automation window or Set default changed since (proposed departure below); OK/Apply save both files and install. Set default resets the bindings in memory, the scripts' too, and installs and saves nothing (the next save writes them: OK with a change, a gesture, the automation window's OK); the list keeps its modified flag. The gesture counts every binding holding the keys, itself included ("already exists for" its own name), only the same window as doubled, Switch gives the others the old keys of (id, chosen window), the mapping takes the names table's name, the audio file is saved only when the Audio window was chosen or named; menu items offer the window choice (Shift alone), mapped buttons do not (Shift held). Routing as SetAccels: in the Line editor the text field's own keys (Delete, Backspace, arrows with Shift/Ctrl, Ctrl+A/C/V/X, Enter, numpad digits unless TEXT_FIELD_ALLOW_NUMPAD_HOTKEYS) come first and plain Enter always commits and goes to the next line, numpad Enter / Ctrl+numpad Enter are fixed; the Grid's fixed Ctrl+C/X/V come first, Video's play/seek bindings (Space, L, ;) act in the Grid too, a Grid action bound for the Editor window is dropped (legacy's unreachable branch), hidden tag buttons have no hotkey (the count read when installed), a window's binding takes its key before the application's shortcuts and even for an action the rewrite does not have yet; GetHKey reads "Num -" as the '-' key. GRID_FILTER_IGNORE_IN_ACTIONS through a hotkey sets "ignore filtering" to the opposite of the stored option (after Set default it turns the kept setting on again), the menu item toggles what the Grid shows. Mapped buttons' tooltips: "<name> (<keys>)" and "Shortcut can be set using Shift + Click". Approved departures: C04-short-file, the registry's lists are read as stored whatever their count (legacy loads the defaults over a file of ten bindings or fewer); the script bindings keep S2's macro identity (S44-macro-alias) and are written back by legacy name. Approved departures (docs/qt/compatibility-decisions.md): O2-reset-history (R3): ClearList keeps the list's history position, so after Set default following edits in the same dialog the next edit erases, and Undo reads, past the history's end (undefined behaviour); the rewrite restarts the history with the list. O2-window-letter (R3): legacy writes a binding whose window letter it could not read with "GSEVA" indexed by -1; the rewrite's lists never hold one and skip it. O2-stale-copy: each Options dialog edits a copy of its own and a cancelled dialog's edits go with it (legacy's static hotkeysCopy outlived the dialog, so a later OK/Apply wrote them and reverted bindings changed since; `shell_tests` hotkeysMappingRefusesAndAsks). Integrated with A2-A4 (2026-10-04): the Audio window's bindings are routed (HotkeysController::actionFor window 4, and the Grid's Audio bindings but AUDIO_COMMIT to AUDIO_NEXT; an _ALT id answers as its main id) and the audio box's buttons are mapped buttons (tooltip and Shift+click); `shell_tests` audioHotkeysFollowTheRegistry. Differences: Qt key presses become wx key codes, shifted punctuation as the US layout's base key (wx reports the actual layout's unshifted key); AudioHotkeys.txt's bindings are read at startup with the main ones (legacy reads them when the Options dialog or the audio box first opens; every change to an audio binding saves them); the tooltip shows the installed binding. Review fixes (2026-10-04). Routing: every Global binding is in the shell's table (HikariSubFrame::SetAccels, HikariSubFrame.cpp:1754-1800; HotkeysController::globalShortcuts, Main.qml runGlobalHotkey), the menu items' included, from a floating panel too; keys bound twice go to the first in map order, the scripts' (S2) give way to a static binding of the same keys, and F6/Shift+F6 to any Global binding; the focused panel's own bindings come first. An action id below 5000 bound for the Global window runs in its own window (OnUseWindowHotkey, :1802-1814): an Audio one only with an audio box, an _ALT id as its main id and only while that id has a Global binding (GetHKey's id + 10, :376). A Global id bound for a panel runs the frame's command (the event goes up to the frame). GLOBAL_PREVIOUS_FRAME/GLOBAL_NEXT_FRAME (Left/Right) step frames from any panel that does not take the keys (the video panel has no arrow keys of its own, as VideoBox::OnKeyPress has none); VIDEO_STOP runs from the Video window's bindings. The Line editor's controls other than its text fields route the Editor bindings (EditBox's table, TabPanel::SetAccels) except the commit keys. CheckLastKeyEvent (config.cpp:1033-1045): the same action again within 100 ms (50 in the video) is ignored in OnMenuSelected1's ids, the Grid's, the audio box's and the video's accelerators. P6's tab keys (GLOBAL_ADD_PAGE, GLOBAL_NEXT_TAB, GLOBAL_PREVIOUS_TAB, GLOBAL_CLOSE_PAGE), the Last session items and every other menu action are registry bindings; no Classic shortcut is hard-coded but F6/Shift+F6 (the rewrite's panel cycling). Keypad: FillTable's "Num 0".."Num 9", "Num .", "Num +", "Num *", "Num /", "Num Enter" are the keypad's keys (Qt's KeypadModifier; a comma decimal key is "Num ."); with Num Lock off its keys have no FillTable name. Mapping: OnMapHkey's Switch is order-identical (Hotkeys.cpp:497-509: each doubled binding is cleared, then given (id, window)'s keys as they are then, so those after the mapped binding get none). GetHKey's 'Shortcut "%s" is invalid' (Hotkeys.cpp:372-375; a key of more than one UTF-16 unit that is no FillTable name) goes to the log window when the bindings install. LoadHkeys's numeric label (a sign and digits, wxString::IsNumber; Hotkeys.cpp:300-301) is read with each legacy build's wxAtoi on its platform (R5-per-platform, hotkeyLabelNumber; both pinned on every host): the Windows build's _wtoi clamps past the int range to INT_MAX / INT_MIN, the Linux build's atoi truncates a 64-bit strtol (core::legacy::atoi; "4294971297" is GRID_HIDE_LAYER there), never throwing; one in the scripts' range has no name and is dropped at the next save (legacy writes it as "=<keys>", which it cannot read back). Gesture: every main-menu item with a Global id (OnMenuSelected/OnMenuSelected1; a checkable one is not switched, MenuDialog::SendEvent), every Grid menu item with an id from 4000 (SubsGrid.cpp:294-302, for the Subtitles window), the macros (OnMapHkey(-1, "Script <file>-<n>"), no window choice, Automation.cpp:1376-1379), the video panel's Play / Pause, Play the current line and Stop (BitmapButton.cpp:97-106, 121-136: Shift held, the window choice, "<name> (<keys>)" and the gesture line as the tooltip), and the audio box's two-hotkey buttons with Ctrl held (MappedButton.cpp:437-445). Approved departure O2-second-hotkey: Ctrl+click on a two-hotkey button maps the second hotkey, the main id - 10 (legacy computed that id and passed GetId(), mapping the main one). The Options list: the gutter while filtered (HikariListCtrl CheckIfHasHiddenBlock/ShowOrHideBlock, HikariListCtrl.cpp:968-1001: "+" where rows are hidden, "-" where a block was shown) and a cell whose text does not fit shows it whole as its tooltip (ItemText, the Function column's first 1000 characters; ItemHotkey). Global actions the rewrite does not have take their keys and do nothing, each waiting for its card (GLOBAL_OPEN_FONT_COLLECTOR now opens the font collector, [Y8](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/191); GLOBAL_VIDEO_ZOOM and GLOBAL_RESET_VIDEO_ZOOM now run, [V4](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/183); GLOBAL_HIDE_TAGS, GLOBAL_PREVIOUS_LINE/GLOBAL_NEXT_LINE and GLOBAL_REMOVE_TEXT now run, [E6](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/188)): GLOBAL_SET_START_TIME/GLOBAL_SET_END_TIME (Ctrl+Left/Right), GLOBAL_OPEN_DUMMY_VIDEO, GLOBAL_SELECT_FROM_VIDEO (F2), GLOBAL_SNAP_WITH_START/GLOBAL_SNAP_WITH_END (Shift+Left/Right, OnAudioSnap) (wave 5, video remainder); GLOBAL_AUTOMATION_LOAD_LAST_SCRIPT (not in the rewrite's Automation menu) (wave 5, not yet carded); GLOBAL_EDITOR and GLOBAL_VIEW_ALL..GLOBAL_VIEW_SUBS run since [D2](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/201); as in legacy, GLOBAL_SAVE_WITH_VIDEO_NAME and GLOBAL_VIDEO_INDEXING change nothing from a key (OnMenuSelected reads the item's check without switching it), nor do the submenu ids (GLOBAL_SORT_LINES, GLOBAL_SORT_SELECTED_LINES, GLOBAL_RECENT_*). The Video window's actions the rewrite does not have take their keys too (VIDEO_PREVIOUS_FILE/NEXT_FILE, the chapters, VIDEO_COPY_COORDS, VIDEO_DELETE_FILE: wave 5, video remainder; VIDEO_VOLUME_PLUS/MINUS, VIDEO_HIDE_PROGRESS_BAR, VIDEO_ASPECT_RATIO and VIDEO_SAVE_* now run, V4 #183; VIDEO_FULL_SCREEN, and GLOBAL_EDITOR in fullscreen (VideoBox::OpenEditor), now run, [V5](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/184)). Differences: the invalid-key message is logged once per binding at each install (legacy once per accelerator table it goes to: the frame's, each tab's, the audio box's); a key of one non-ASCII character is valid for legacy but has no Qt sequence here; Qt's shortcut map also tries a keypad key without its KeypadModifier, so with Num Lock off a Global binding of an arrow, Home, End, PgUp, PgDn, Insert or Delete takes the keypad's key too (legacy's WXK_NUMPAD_* have no name); a disabled menu item takes no click, so the gesture cannot map it (legacy maps it with Shift). The video context menu's gesture (VideoBox.cpp:1028-1033) came with the menu in V4 (#183). Not yet: legacy's toolbar (no gesture there; the rewrite has no toolbar); the two Last session check items (ids outside IDS, which legacy maps under an empty name); Exit and Show / Hide log window run on Shift+click (legacy's DisableMapping makes the click do nothing); Enter in the Line editor's time fields as EDITBOX_COMMIT_GO_NEXT_LINE with EDITBOX_DONT_GO_TO_NEXT_LINE_ON_TIMES_EDIT (EditBox::OnNewline, EditBox.cpp:987-999; wave 5, editor remainder); the importer of Hotkeys.txt / AudioHotkeys.txt (wave 5, O3 #195). Windows: Windows CI [run 37240819196](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/actions/runs/37240819196) and Winix VM job-2866cbab23eb493883406c28656eee4a on 80f3fe44 (859 tests, 0 failures) |

ambiguous encoding needs Latin-1 or Windows-1252, UTF-16 BOMs read. `hikari_ui_settings_import_tests` (13 functions, 25 cases): roots listed once each, never combined; the snapshot keeps bytes, hashes, encoding and header (CRLF folded on every platform, and the fixture's CRLF brace table imported), the same bytes reuse it, the legacy files are never written; QML uses `pragma Translator` with `qsTr`, and `qsTranslate` for a string of another surface (56 files; `Main.qml` is `HikariSub.MainWindow` and names the surface of each region it hosts). C++ uses `QCoreApplication::translate` and `QT_TRANSLATE_NOOP` with a literal context; no `tr()` remains. On Linux it reports "1853 messages in 30 contexts, 476 sources: every context is registered". With a `QObject::tr` in a C++ file and a `qsTranslate("Planted", ...)` in a QML file planted, it failed with four problems, each naming its file and line, and passed again once they were removed. `hikari_i18n_contexts_tests` (8, now 12): the registry reader, the lint on bad and clean fixtures (raw strings, digit separators, comments), the lint on computed texts without marks, the line-order pairing and the moves of entries without a call, the map file round trip, the join and the refusals of disagreeing entries, the comparison naming every difference, the checked-in catalogs using only registered contexts and holding the Grid headings, the rewrite's own copy in sentence case ("line", "document" and "untitled" lower case inside a sentence; legacy ids keep their text), and the DirectShow open messages marked. `hikari_i18n_contexts_keep_translations` (ctest): each catalog before the move (its blob at 3e06caa9) against the one O6 committed, through the map. Review fix: the Grid's column headings, a table of plain literals that lupdate never extracted, are `QT_TRANSLATE_NOOP("HikariSub.Grid", ...)`; `hikari_lupdate` then found 1862 texts (9 new `HikariSub.Grid` keys, unfinished, 3 with same-text fill) in each catalog, and `CheckedIn.CatalogsHoldTheGridHeadings` (the ninth test in `hikari_i18n_contexts_tests`) failed with 36 missing keys on the catalogs before and passes after; the headings then took legacy's translations of their ids, as O4's exact rows ("Original text" too): ten `HikariSub.Grid` keys added to `migration/keys.ts`, `keymap.tsv` regenerated (812 exact, 1132 obsolete; `CheckedIn.KeyMapIsTheCandidateMapOfTheRecordedInputs`), `convert` into a scratch directory giving `report.md` and the ten entries, finished, in each catalog (`CheckedIn.ConversionReproducesTheCatalogs`; `CatalogsHoldTheGridHeadings` checks every heading finished and translated); the lint also asks a file that looks up a computed text (`translate`/`qsTranslate` with a non-literal source) for marks with that context (`ContextLint.AComputedTextNeedsItsContextMarkedInTheFile`; today's sources have eight such lookups, all beside their tables), and the DirectShow adapter's failed-open message is translated through `application_directshow.cpp`'s marked table, which holds every text the adapter reports (`CheckedIn.DirectShowOpenMessagesAreMarked`) | Joined keys: per language, 18 unfinished entries that lupdate had filled with the text of another context now share a key with a finished entry of the same text, so they show it (for example `HikariSub.Video` "Pause" in full screen, `HikariSub.Colours` "Primary color" in the simple picker, `HikariSub.Workspace` "Close"). Legacy's context-free gettext showed the same; these await translator review. O6 did not run `hikari_i18n_keymap`, which re-extracts today's keys: carrying legacy translations into the keys added after O4 was a translation decision. The user took it on 2026-10-13 ([O4-post-keys-carried](compatibility-decisions.md)): the map paired 1314 keys (1253 exact, 61 placeholder) instead of 834, the 15 joined keys among the exact rows with the same translations, and after the re-carry from legacy 0.0.1 (2026-10-13 afternoon) 1326 (1264 exact, 62 placeholder), every exact row finished. Closing evidence at 3291e376, before both carries; neither carry has a Windows run yet (closed 2026-10-13; Linux CI run 38265137408 (Ubuntu, offscreen and xvfb) and 38265137460 (Fedora), Windows CI run 38265137413, Winix VM job-9b9f1f3dd1e349f093b6434aae491978 (full test: 1346 tests, 0 failed, 4 skipped)): on the Winix VM `hikari_ui_language_tests` semanticContextsShowTheCatalogOnSurfaces passed with 1286 shown translations in 21 contexts, the same counts as Linux (job-4779a0010d964dbb969c7a2a074f1c0e), and all 40 i18n tests ran with 38 passed (hikari_i18n_contexts_are_semantic, the ContextLint and CheckedIn tests and the Qm tests among them) and 2 skipped (Qm.GettextCatalogsMatchTheLegacyMoLookup without msgfmt, hikari_i18n_contexts_keep_translations without git objects; job-ce14239b04a34e6181d2e840180765d5); `ctest -L i18n` there selects only 4 tests because CMake 4.3.1-msvc1 drops the gtest labels (job-cf993e72cdbe4f47bc84be750548fb34; CI's CMake 4.4 keeps them). The CI runs passed the lint, ContextLint, CheckedIn, Qm and language tests and skip hikari_i18n_contexts_keep_translations (a shallow clone has no 3e06caa9 blobs); it passed at 3291e376 on akari-rvm (ubuntu-x64-release-remote, 21 i18n tests, 0 failed). nothing for a cancelled index; "An error occurred when creating audio source: %s"; "An error occurred when converting audio: %s") with FFMS2's text, late answers dropped. `indexed_source_tests` (+9): the helper's Probe lists the tracks' names, languages and codecs without indexing; an Indices folder that cannot be written (read-only on Linux; on Windows a folder in the index file's place) makes the video's helper hand the index over in a temporary file outside it, which the box's helper opens without indexing, removed when released, when the source opens again (none when the index is read or there is no audio track) and when the source ends. `video_session_tests` (+1): legacy's Stopped state (Stop while playing, until Play); the open's chosen track and index file reach the source, and its handed-over index file is kept until the next open or close, and general playback selects the chosen track (its place among the audio tracks), also when the same file is reopened with another track. `shell_tests` (+9), real media helper: a file's mixdown and its peaks per column at zooms 100, 50 and 10 computed from the fixture's formula, the cache file's name and its size once closed, the active Line's start mark, the new display focused, the cursor following the mouse over the waveform but not the ruler, rendered pixels (the start boundary over the waveform, a waveform column, the background above it) in the software adaptation (offscreen) and in RHI (xvfb; another file replaces it, Open audio from video, a video without audio closes the box; "Choose the track" on a two-track file opens the chosen track; for a video it asks before the open video is replaced, Cancel leaves the open video as it was (legacy RendererFFMS2::OpenFile's "safe mode"), and a chosen track becomes the video's audioTrack and the box's track; with index and cache folders the video's open writes `Indices/clip_1.ffindex`, the box's first cache is made, and on reopening the video the index is read and the box reads its cache back; dropping files with a video open trims the cache folder to 10 by last access, the cache in use kept although read longest ago; the trim keeps the cache in use under its final name and as .part, a limit below 1 keeps everything; an audio-only file given to Open video opens in the box and the video stays; with an Indices folder that cannot be written the box opens the video's audio from the handed-over index without indexing, and the temporary file goes once the box has opened | Kept from legacy: a shade one column wide at the selection's edge for an inactive Line wholly on one side of it; the end boundary's flags in the start boundary's colour; keyframes drawn at ((ms - 20) / 10) * 10, truncated towards zero, and the walk ending at the first keyframe past the view; zoomed-out columns taking every sample of the peak blocks they touch; the ruler's label spacing measured between the second and third second boundaries; the view's position kept when another file opens in the same box; the blank audio's name added to the recent list and pruned as a missing file; nothing reported for a file without audio or a cancelled index, and FFMS_CreateIndexer's and FFMS_GetAudio's errors only as debug messages (qDebug; legacy HikariLogDebug shows them in debug builds only); a positive delay dropping the audio's last frames; a negative delay's last frames, past legacy's reduced sample count, not decoded (silence in mono; in stereo half silence and half the block buffer's earlier frames); old caches trimmed by last access time when files open with a video open (legacy OpenFile, OpenFiles and LoadLastSession through VideoBox::DeleteAudioCache; here when a video becomes ready, after Open file and after dropped files), while the box is opening once its cache file is known; the chooser for a video's tracks asked before indexing, as legacy asked inside the indexing progress before FFMS_DoIndexing2; the video indexed with the chosen audio track alone (none without audio, index file `<name>_-1.ffindex`). The Windows Direct3D drawing (DoUpdateImage) is the normative look; the wx Linux fallback (DrawWithWx) is non-normative where it differs: ruler ticks and labels without the label-spacing rule, the video mark drawn while playing too, another draw order. R5-per-platform: the cache trim never removes the cache in use (legacy on Windows failed to remove an open file and went on to the next oldest). Approved departures (R3-hang-crash-loss, extended to A1 by the user on 2026-10-04, "Extend R3": a failed cache block, cache create/write errors, a short cache read, "Not enough memory to cache the audio", the helper lost while open): in a RAM cache the part of a failed block FFMS2 did not write is silence (an undefined read: legacy's new block was uninitialised memory; the frames decoded before the failure are kept as legacy kept them); a RAM cache that cannot be allocated ends the open with "Not enough memory to cache the audio" (legacy crashed), a disk cache that cannot be created or written ends it with "Cannot create the audio cache <file>" / "Cannot write the audio cache <file>" (legacy drew an empty progress bar forever or cached nothing); a media helper that ends while opening or caching ends the open with "Cannot open audio <file>" (legacy's in-process FFMS2 took the program down); a short read from the cache file is silence (stale data, not an undefined read: legacy GetBuffer read into a thread_local buffer it reused, so a short fread left an earlier read's samples, which depended on the redraw history). Approved departure A1-trim-in-use (R3, 2026-10-04): on Linux the trim no longer unlinks the cache in use (legacy's Linux build removed the open cache and its rename then failed, so the cache was lost). Equivalent, not a difference: every Open video lists the file's tracks in the video's helper first (FFMS_CreateIndexer, nothing indexed), as legacy's provider did at the start of its own open; a failed index-file write is legacy's debug message "Cannot save index, error %s occurred" on the helper's stderr, and then (decided 2026-10-04) the video's helper hands the new index to the box in a temporary file (made exclusively in the temp folder by the host), so the box never indexes the video's track again, as legacy's box shared the video's index in memory; the file is removed once the box has opened from it, and otherwise when the video's source opens again or ends (only if that file cannot be written either does the box index the track again). Decided 2026-10-04, kept: without a settings folder (tests) there are no index files; callers that open a video without an index request (the automation shell port, the transport coordinator) still index every audio track. Settings (O1 registry): AUDIO_RECENT_FILES is recent.audio (written at each addition, kept through Set default as HikariSubFrame keeps it); AUDIO_RAM_CACHE, AUDIO_DELAY, AUDIO_CACHE_FILES_LIMIT and ACCEPTED_AUDIO_STREAM are audio.ramCache, audio.delay, audio.cacheFilesLimit and video.acceptedAudioStream, shown on the Options dialog's Audio, Audio > Advanced and Video pages and read when the box opens or trims its cache, as the legacy provider reads Options, so a dialog change reaches the next open (`shell_tests` spellingAndAudioOptionsComeFromTheRegistry). Not yet: the Script Info "Audio File" association, the ruler's right-click mark (A3), the editor's uncommitted times (the box follows committed ones), the display options and colours from the settings registry (legacy defaults and the dark theme; AudioDisplay::ChangeOptions after the Options dialog), the antialiased cursor, fonts by name (the application font at legacy's relative sizes, bold where legacy's were), the Open audio dialog's fallback to the latest recent video's folder (the rewrite keeps no recent video list), Ctrl+click on a recent entry to show it in its folder (no select-in-folder exists yet), Windows observations |

the upper half of the palette is the inner colour; the lines are unevenly spaced across sub-caches when overlaps do not divide 2048; the speech layout's top rows all read band 1023 once the bands run out; a wheel zoom past the slider's range stores the value as it is (one notch past) and a new box clamps it; partial notches (under 120) neither zoom nor change the vertical zoom but scroll in proportion; Ctrl with Shift is a Shift wheel; linking copies the vertical zoom into the volume, never the reverse, and a box made with the link on rewrites AUDIO_VOLUME; auto-scroll is read from the settings at each Line change, the switches from them when a box is made. Equivalent, not a difference: legacy's workers transformed sub-caches past the view (numThreads x len); the rewrite transforms the view's sub-caches only, with legacy's budget, and keeps the picture until the view changes (legacy rendered it again on every full redraw, with the same pixels). Approved departures (docs/qt/compatibility-decisions.md): A2-tall-spectrum (R3: a display 1024 rows or taller read band 1024 of a 1024-band line, past its end, for its top row; the rewrite reads zero there) and A2-linked-thumb (the linked vertical zoom and volume sliders both show the exact value; legacy's thumb-pixel copy could show the volume one off). Integrated with A3, A4 and O2 (2026-10-04): one spectrum module (`audio_spectrum.h`) serves the display's RenderRange and aegisub.get_frequency_peaks (legacy CreateRange at overlaps 1 through the same sub-caches, a range read 64 sub-caches at a time; the FrequencyPeaks and Lua helper tests unchanged), with one legacy FFT built without floating-point contraction on every compiler (-ffp-contract=off on GCC/Clang, /fp:precise on MSVC, and the file's own `#pragma fp_contract(off)` / `STDC FP_CONTRACT OFF`), so its bits are legacy's on MSVC too; the volume slider sets the player's volume (AUDIO_VOLUME when the box's audio is set, legacy AudioBox::SetFile, then each move of the volume or of the linked vertical zoom); the playhead is kept in view during playback (A4's UpdateTimer, which legacy runs whatever AUDIO_AUTO_SCROLL says); AUDIO_SCROLL_RIGHT / AUDIO_SCROLL_LEFT are the hotkey registry's Audio bindings in the box and the Grid (O2; `shell_tests` audioHotkeysFollowTheRegistry: A remapped to K, K scrolls in both and A no longer does); the spectrum colours are settings (audio.spectrumBackground #000000, audio.spectrumEcho #674FD7, audio.spectrumInner #F4F4F4: legacy theme colours, so with theme files excluded they are the rewrite's own settings) listed on the Options dialog's Themes page in legacy's rows ("Audio spectrum background", "Audio spectrum echo", "Audio spectrum"; a double click picks the colour), saved by OK/Apply and read again by the box (ChangeOptions, AudioSpectrum::ChangeColours), left alone by Set default (`shell_tests` spectrumColoursAreThemeSettings: the picture equals a fresh render with the new palette); a horizontal wheel acts as the vertical one (legacy reads the rotation, not the axis) with the Windows sign: a tilt to the right is +120 to legacy and -120 in Qt's x (`shell_tests` audioBoxZoomScrollAndGain); the switches sit in the button row in ButtonSizer's order; the default arrangement gives the box legacy's AUDIO_BOX_HEIGHT (170 px for display, search bar and buttons at the default 1280 x 800 window; A1's pixel test checks it). Approved departure A2-theme-colours: the Themes page lists only these three colours, editable (legacy edited a theme's colours only after copying a built-in theme). Not yet: the video's volume through the box (RendererFFMS2's ABox->SetVolume/GetVolume), karaoke's zoom changes (A5, OnKaraoke), HikariSlider's own behaviour (the wheel moving the thumb 3 pixels, page steps of a tenth, right- or Shift-click jumps, arrow keys by 3), the other theme colours (A1's display colours stay legacy's dark-theme defaults), the legacy Linux build's spectrum drawing (DrawWithWx, non-normative as A1's), button icons (text labels) and a legacy runtime capture of the spectrum. Spix `hikari_ui_audio_timing_workflow`: three Lines timed by keyboard (C, V, Enter each), the append on the last, Z, Z, Right and B | Kept legacy quirks: AUDIO_COMMIT always goes to the next Line (legacy CommitChanges(true) never reads AUDIO_NEXT_LINE_ON_COMMIT; its toggle only saves the option); the Line NextLine appends on the last Line copies the Line at the key equal to the number of shown Lines before the last (GetElementByKey read as a key), not the last Line, when Lines are hidden; that append names the single step "Adding a new line"; Alt-dragging the start on the first Line moves the active Line's own end (CopyDialogueWithOffset(0, -1) is Line 0); a box commit on the same Line keeps the box's times and runs no SetDialogue (lead-in values not on 10 ms stay so in the box); the selection may end before it starts ("Negative time"). E63-invalid-commit applies to the box's commits as to the editor's. Approved departures (docs/qt/compatibility-decisions.md): A3-adjacent-step (without AUDIO_AUTO_COMMIT, or when nothing else changed, the Alt release's neighbour change is its own "Changing time on audio spectrum" step; legacy changed the Line with no step, so it joined the next one), A3-snap-bounds (R3: with no Line shown, legacy's snap walk read GetDialogue(-1); the walk stays in the file). Integrated with A2, A4 and O2 (2026-10-04): the box's keys are the hotkey registry's Audio bindings (AudioBox::SetAccels; an _ALT id sends its main id, legacy GetHKey's id + 10), and the Grid takes the Audio bindings but AUDIO_COMMIT to AUDIO_NEXT (TabPanel::SetAccels; SubsGrid::OnAccelerator hands them to the box): `shell_tests` audioHotkeysFollowTheRegistry (AUDIO_COMMIT remapped to J commits and Enter no longer does; X and Z in the Grid); the box's buttons show their bindings in the tooltip (two for the _ALT pairs) and map them by Shift+click; the playback hooks are the box's own: Play after Next/Previous unless AUDIO_DONT_PLAY_WHEN_LINE_CHANGES (AudioDisplay::Next/Prev's GetTimesSelection), the middle double click's Play, the player's SetEndPosition while a boundary is dragged and not playing to the end, the ruler's mark feeding AUDIO_PLAY_BEFORE_MARK / AFTER_MARK (`shell_tests` audioBoxPlaybackModes sets it by a right click on the ruler); GLOBAL_SET_AUDIO_FROM_VIDEO / GLOBAL_SET_AUDIO_MARK_FROM_VIDEO are enabled with the box and the editor (legacy OnMenuOpened: ABox != nullptr && editor; the editor switch is D2's, its editor the editing target's: `shell_tests` setAudioFromVideoNeedsTheBoxAndTheEditor). Checked: a commit with the Line's own draft pending carries every field the draft holds (text, translation, times, margins) to each selected Line as EditBox::Send's cells (`CommitTest.TheLinesOwnDraftGoesWithTheCommitFieldByField`); the Line editor has no Comment, Layer, Style, Actor or Effect field yet, so no draft holds them. Equivalent, not a difference: legacy Commit ran EditBox::OnEdit after an auto-commit unless DISABLE_LIVE_VIDEO_EDITING (the video's preview of the edit); the rewrite's video shows the committed Document and the box's auto-commit is a commit, so the video takes the new times with video.disableLiveEditing on or off (`shell_tests` audioAutoCommitReachesTheVideo). Not yet: karaoke (A5); timing while the audio is still loading (the mouse acts once it is ready); the editor's frame display (TimeCtrl with frames) and MicroDVD / MPL2 field precision (milliseconds are kept); the display options from the registry (A1's). Done with O2: Audio bindings made for the Global window and the second binding's Ctrl+click (O2-second-hotkey; `shell_tests` audioBindingsForTheGlobalWindowAndCtrlClick). a start past the audio plays its last frame's position, nothing; Stop while not playing plays from where the last Stop stopped to the last play's end (0 to 5000 ms in a new box, a position past the end plays nothing), and a playback that ended by itself leaves that memory alone; Mechanism (PortAudio, ADR 0010), not behaviour: the cursor's position is the output's clock estimate, the frame being heard (legacy Windows read DirectSound's play cursor, without the DAC latency); a device that refuses the audio's rate gets linear resampling (legacy's DirectSound and GStreamer resampled in the system); the default output device; failures are logged as legacy's player logged them ("Failed creating audio playback device.", "Audio player failed.") and the next play opens the device again. Approved departures (docs/qt/compatibility-decisions.md): A4-play-undecoded (R3: frames not yet decoded while the box is still loading play as silence; legacy read its RAM cache's uninitialised blocks or a short disk read's stale buffer), A4-linux-player (both platforms behave as the Windows build: the cursor from the output clock and the silence tail; legacy's Linux build used GStreamer), A4-wasapi-default (on Windows the box's output opens WASAPI's default device, and audio.outputHostApi on the Options dialog's Audio page, Windows only, switches to DirectSound; a change takes effect when the output next opens: at once while idle, after the playback while playing; `shell_tests` audioOutputFollowsTheHostApiSetting records the host API each output is made with; elsewhere the setting does nothing). Integrated with A2, A3 and O2 (2026-10-04): Play pauses a playing video before it looks for the audio (AudioDisplay::Play); AUDIO_PLAY_LINE_ALT runs AUDIO_PLAY_LINE (GetHKey's id + 10, so GetTimesSelection's karaoke flag is id == AUDIO_PLAY_LINE for both); the keys are the hotkey registry's Audio bindings in the box and the Grid (`shell_tests` audioHotkeysFollowTheRegistry: AUDIO_PLAY_LINE_ALT remapped to Y plays the line from the box and the Grid, R no longer does); the volume slider's volume (A2); A3's playback hooks (Next/Previous, the middle double click, SetEndPosition while dragging, the ruler's mark); the play buttons in ButtonSizer's order. The player fills the output on a thread of its own, as legacy's DirectSoundPlayer2Thread did (every 5 ms while playing; the owner's calls and the fill serialized by one lock, the output's real-time callback taking neither and allocating nothing), and holds the audio it plays until it stops; the box's cached audio locks its store for that reader (`audio_box_player_tests` TheFillThreadFeedsTheOutputWhileTheOwnerIsBusy: an owner thread blocked 700 ms, past the 250 ms queued, causes no underrun; ThePlayedAudioIsHeldUntilTheStop); the output without a device renders what is due at each call instead of on the owner's event loop. VideoBox::Pause's audio-box play of the Line when there is no video and indexing is on; the editor's uncommitted times; a device choice beyond the host API; legacy's CheckLastKeyEvent (a repeated audio key within its interval ignored); Windows runs and real-device observation (N6/H2) |

The zoom's menus, wheel and keys and the aspect override's dialog came in V4 (#183), which reaches the view this card implemented and the capture checks. The video context menu's VIDEO_COPY_COORDS item came in V4 too; the hotkey has no default key (legacy's). The rail shows K1's icons ([#204](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/204)) in place of legacy's bitmaps. UI polish (branch w5-polish, 2026-10-05, the user's rule that tool strips are icon-only; [visual-language.md](ux/visual-language.md#tool-strips-option-rows-and-labels)): the rail is a 32-pixel strip of icon-only tool buttons (the family's name its tooltip and accessible name, a family without its tool saying so in the tooltip instead of being drawn faded, the checked family the style's, Tab-reachable with Up/Down between them); the strip below the video carries only the active family's options, editable values and the batch picker, and is absent for the crosshair and for a family without its tool; the batch picker is an icon button (pick-lines) checked while Lines are picked, with Clear (clear) only then, and the "Targets: ..." readout is gone; read-only values (the crosshair's position, the scale) are a readout beside the video times. `shell_tests` visualToolRailCrosshairAndCopyCoordinates checks each family button icon-only (the Icon its content, IconOnly display, tooltip and accessible name the family's name, Tab focus, at most 32 wide), no family name drawn on the 40-pixel-at-most rail, no strip for the crosshair and no visualBatch item; visualGesturesCommitOnReleaseAndEscCancels the picker's icon, name, checked state and count tooltip and Clear shown only with a batch. Families 1-10 select no tool until T2-T6 land. A non-ASS rail stays disabled with the crosshair, as legacy; legacy's renderer also drew no crosshair for such a Document. The Protected reference refuses the tools' writes in the shell only by never being their target: the shell's reference EditSession is not built protected (DocumentFiles), so the Gesture's own Protected refusal is unit-tested only. Video first, then ASS subtitles: opening subtitles into the empty window closes its video here (VideoController::offer on the new editing target), so that order is not observed in the shell; the rule above does not depend on it. Not reproduced, a proposed departure: after a Document became non-ASS with a video open (Visual -1), legacy EditBox::OnCommit's SetVisual made Visuals::Get's default, a Position tool, for that Document (EditBox.cpp:979, Visuals.cpp:76-78); here a non-ASS Document has no tool. The pointer over the picture only (2026-10-14, branch qt-videocursor, the user's rule, with legacy's fix of the same bug, a8a428e4c "Show the position pointer only over the video picture"): the crosshair hides the pointer and is drawn only on the picture, VideoView::onPicture, the video rectangle with its right and bottom edges outside (legacy's IsOnVideoFrame, VideoFrameArea.h); over the letterbox and pillarbox bars the pointer is the arrow and nothing is drawn. Before, the pointer was hidden over the whole video area, bars included, as legacy's VideoBox.cpp:607-611 hid it over the window, so over the bars no pointer showed at all; and the cross was drawn on the rectangle's right and bottom edges, which the legacy probe's capture still records (VisualCapture.ReplaysTheLegacyProbe now expects those five points off the picture). `visual_tools_tests` HiddenWithoutVideoAndAfterLeaveOrRightRelease checks onPicture at each edge, on the bars, the panel and without a video, and the cross off a bar. The tools also hear the pointer leave the video area in fullscreen: the overlay's MouseArea lost its hover state when it moved into the fullscreen window (the main window's leave reached it there) and never sent the Leave, so the cross stayed drawn while the pointer was on the fullscreen panel; a HoverHandler on the overlay sends it (once, not during a drag). `shell_tests` (+1) videoPointerIsTheToolsOnlyOverThePicture, real mouse moves and the window's cursor: windowed with the panel's own bars, a 7:1 picture (letterbox) and a 7:10 one (pillarbox), zoomed by the wheel off the centre, the zoom mode, fullscreen with "Show toolbar" zoomed and not, and the player layout; each time in and out of the picture (its centre, corners and two pixels inside each edge with a bar, the bars' middles and three pixels outside), the hidden pointer and the drawn cross inside, the arrow and no cross outside; every family (the tool's own pointer inside, never the hidden one, the arrow on the bars); the seek bar, Play, Next frame, the volume and the rail's buttons (the times field not the hidden pointer), and in fullscreen the seek bar, Play, "Show toolbar", the volume and a tool button take the arrow coming from the picture. |

Approved departure T3-release-no-change (User, 2026-10-12, one rule with T6-no-change-no-step): legacy committed on every release, so RotationZ's release after the two-point angle's first point and after a click off both points (rotz-two-points steps 1 and 5), and Scale's release of a drag inside its rectangle that left the text as it was (scale-rectangle step 4), sent the unchanged text and made an undo step (and marked the file modified), and a several-Line release did so for every target; the rewrite opens a gesture on the click and cancels it on a release that changes no Line's text, on the one-Line and the several-Line paths, so no step is recorded and the Document stays unmodified (`VisualTransformEdit.AReleaseThatChangesNoLineRecordsNothing`). The video shows a gesture's staged text through the preview shared with T2 (`Gesture::applyTo`, `setPreview`, 60b43eada); no T3 test reads the video during a drag. Pointer shapes, ported at the user's 2026-10-13 decision (not a departure; the mechanism as T2's): Scale's shapes over the arrows' ends while hovering (the common end SIZING, the width's SIZEWE, the height's SIZENS, each set only from the arrow as legacy's HasArrow test did, VisualScale.cpp:286-297), the dragged axis' shape from a press (left SIZEWE, right SIZENS, middle, Shift or "Preserve proportions" SIZING, :300-312), "Scale to rectangle"'s edge and corner shapes over both rectangles (:152-172), the arrow on the release; RotationZ's SIZING from a press, from the second point in the two-point mode (VisualRotationZ.cpp:211, 215); RotationXY's SIZEWE, SIZENS or SIZING by the button (VisualRotationXY.cpp:215-217). Esc during a drag puts the arrow back with the rest of the gesture's state: this follows the approved transaction rule (an abandoned gesture leaves nothing behind), so it has no ledger row of its own (User, 2026-10-13). Approved departure T3-scale-shift-hover (User, 2026-10-13): Shift changes Scale's shape only while a button is down; legacy also re-ran the press's shape code on a Shift press or release while hovering and on a release with Shift held (VisualScale.cpp:298-311), so a drag's shape came up over the hover rules'; here the hover rules alone set the shape while hovering and a Shift drag's release leaves the hover shape at once (`VisualTransformPointer.ScaleShiftChangesTheShapeOnlyDuringADrag`; `shell_tests` visualScaleAndRotationsThroughThePanel). Tests: `visual_transform_tests` VisualTransformPointer (ScaleArrowEndsShowTheirAxisShape, ScaleDragsShowTheScaledAxis, ScaleShiftChangesTheShapeOnlyDuringADrag, ScaleRectanglesEdgesAndCornersShowTheirResizeShapes, RotationZDragsShowTheFourWayShape, RotationZTwoPointsShowTheFourWayShapeFromTheSecond, RotationXYDragsShowTheTurnedAxis), on the probe's cases' views; `shell_tests` visualScaleAndRotationsThroughThePanel reads the overlay's cursorShape: SizeHor over the width arrow's end and through its drag, SizeBDiag over the drawn scale rectangle's corner and the arrow inside it, the arrow on a Shift move while hovering, SizeAll on a Shift press and the arrow right after its release with Shift held, SizeAll from RotationZ's second-point press and the arrow after the release, SizeHor through RotationXY's left drag and the arrow after. Linux only (T3-scale-shift-hover, 2026-10-13: the non-UI ctest, 1357 with 9 skipped, and the 58 offscreen UI suites passed; legacy's quirk fails seven of ScaleShiftChangesTheShapeOnlyDuringADrag's checks); no Windows run and no look at the platform's cursor images. Approved departures (User, 2026-10-05, the wave-5 batch-3 review; [compatibility-decisions.md](compatibility-decisions.md)): T3-change-org-end (ChangeOrg replaces a found \org exactly: `{\an7\org(10,20)\frz30}` gives `{\an7\org(40,50)\frz30}`, legacy `{\an7\org(40,50)30}`; `}Other` is kept), T3-org-zoom (the \org drag's offset takes the zoom's scale only, so a zoomed drag no longer jumps by the zoom's offset), T3-scale-vector-clip (Scale keeps a vector clip's scale, `\clip(2,m ...)`, its points scaled in the vector's units), T3-rotz-rect-clip (RotationZ no longer makes a rectangle clip 2 taller on each edit), T3-two-points-change-all (the two-point mode is taken when the family is chosen with "change all" on). Legacy kept (the user's 2026-10-12 decision, not departures): the several-Line path writes the active Line's scale into every target, a click without a drag included (Lines with their own scales take the active Line's); "change all" adds the tag to a first block that lacked it, even when a later block has it. Approved departures (User, 2026-10-12, legacy memory defects): T3-empty-line-border (legacy GetTextSize left the border unset for an empty Line; 0 here), T3-rect-clip-extra-values (a rectangle clip with more than four values wrote past its array; the extra values are dropped here, and so is legacy's "Cannot read clip rectangle values." log). T3-empty-line-border is pinned by `VisualCapture.ReplaysTheLegacyT3MeasureProbe`, T3-rect-clip-extra-values by `VisualTransformText.RectangleClipDropsValuesPastTheFourth`. |

No package is built from the manifest yet (Phase 5): nothing shows a package carries either plugin, so K1 stays unobserved at package level. Differences there: the Document takes the key only once the write succeeded (legacy 20d647c4 kept it after a failed write; legacy main restores the Script Info, SubsGridBase.cpp:433), and it goes into the current undo step only, so an Undo to an earlier step shows the value that step held (legacy's steps shared one Script Info entry). Not part of decision 49: the same legacy code writes "Audio File", "Video File" and "Keyframes File" on save; the rewrite does not. The Document scripts are one list for the application (Automation::ASSScripts), not per Document, and opening another Document adds its scripts after them; a path holding `\|`, `~` or `$` is cut there; relative paths resolve against the working directory (wxFileExists); the menu says nothing when validation fails. Differences: legacy keeps a script in the autoload list and in the Document list as two LuaScripts; the manager keys helpers by path, so a Document script already loaded reloads (its top level runs again) and shares one menu entry; the load is asynchronous, so "Run the last loaded script" waits for the helper; runs are not modal, so its menu item (and the hotkey, which triggers the same Action) is disabled while a macro runs, as the macro items are, and a request still pending when another macro starts (its script was loading) waits for that macro instead of being dropped. The rewrite's menus show no status-bar help, so neither a macro's description nor the help text its validation function returns (second return, COMMAND_DYNAMIC_HELP, Automation.cpp:958-964; legacy shows it in the status bar over the macro's item from the next menu build, Automation.cpp:1374, Menu.cpp:642-645) is shown. Approved departures (docs/qt/compatibility-decisions.md): S4-validation-edits, edits a validation function makes are dropped when it answers false or raises an error, and the Document (content, header, dirty state, history) stays unchanged (legacy AutoToFile edited the file in place and kept them without an undo step); the helper stages nothing and applyMacroResult refuses such a result. S4-run-validation (user decision 2026-10-13): legacy BuildMenu validates every macro when the Automation menu opens and disables the items that answer false (Automation.cpp:1375, 1434), and the capture shows that menu build on every "Run the last loaded script" by its hotkey (OnMenuSelected updates an accelerator's menu first, HikariSubFrame.cpp:680-686), so every Document-script macro with a validation function is validated before the command validates and runs the last one; the rewrite validates no macro when the menu opens (its items stay enabled) and, when a macro runs (menu, script hotkey, "Run the last loaded script"), validates its dependencies' macros and then its own, nothing else. A dependency is a loaded script (autoload or Document script, ready) whose file the running script's top level loaded as Lua code, by require (DependencyControl's requireModules requires) or include, directly or through a file it loaded (dofile and loadfile are nil in the helper; the helper lists them, ScriptInfo::loadedFiles; aegisub.* has no call into another macro, and modules have no validation functions); each such script's macros with a validation function are validated in registration order, the scripts in the order their files were first loaded, in their own helpers on the run's snapshot (Validate: nothing staged, their edits dropped); one unloaded or reloading by its turn is skipped. The first that answers false or raises stops the run as the macro's own validation does, the box naming that script ("Validation Lua script '<dependency>' failed", the error logged first; nothing from the menu) |

Native gate (tools/native-gate step `videofs`, local evidence out/v5/native-gate-r2; sway 1.12 with its default focus_on_window_activation and with `focus`, KWin 6.7.5 `--virtual`, mutter 50.5 `--headless`, Xvfb 21.1 + openbox, Qt 6.11.2), real compositor keys: F, Space, Right and Esc observed on sway, KWin, mutter and X11 with the main window's geometry and the Video panel's focus restored; the context menu's monitor 2 on another output observed on sway (HEADLESS-1, both policies), KWin (Virtual-1 at scale 2), mutter (Meta-1 at scale 2) and X11 (two RandR monitors), the keyboard in the fullscreen window there and back on the Video panel after Esc (on Wayland the window is mapped on the main window's output first and carried back before it hides, so sway's default policy gives and returns the keyboard); the Spix workflow under each compositor: observed. | Approved departure applied: [V5-show-toolbar-icon](compatibility-decisions.md) (User, 2026-10-12): the panel's controls are icon-only with tooltips ("Show toolbar", legacy a check box with its label, is a checkable icon button, K1 role show-toolbar; the five transport buttons were bitmap buttons in legacy too). Platform departures approved 2026-10-06 (wave-5 batch-4 review): [V5-wayland-monitor](compatibility-decisions.md) (on Wayland a client does not know where its windows are, so SetFullscreen(0) takes the monitor the compositor shows the main window on, QWindow::screen, instead of the one holding its centre); [V5-wayland-parent](compatibility-decisions.md) (the fullscreen window has no parent on Wayland (a parent makes it the main window's dialog there, and sway then keeps the keyboard on the main window), and on another output than the main window's it is mapped there first and then made fullscreen on the chosen output (a compositor gives the keyboard only to new windows of its focused output); [V5-monitor-order](compatibility-decisions.md) (the monitors are Qt's screens, whose primary is always first, Windows' MonitorEnumProc1 order; on Linux legacy listed wxDisplay's order, which Qt's equals on Wayland and on X11 when the primary is the first RandR monitor). An empty title shows as the application's name (Qt's fallback). Kept legacy: Esc and B in the fullscreen window only leave (Fullscreen::OnKeyPress returns before VideoBox::OnKeyPress, so B does not minimize and GRID_SET_VISIBLE_LINE_AFTER_FULL_SCREEN applies to the double click only); an open visual gesture is cancelled by Esc first (the accepted T1 policy); the progress bar and the picture's surround keep legacy's white and black on any theme (marks drawn on the video, as V4's zoom dimming); legacy's 200 ms pointer guard (m_ArrowEater) was DirectShow's only. Fixed with the card: the video context menu's recent-file items named their submenu, not the context menu (a delegate's `menu` is the MenuItem's own property), so their triggers had no shell. Not yet: VideoBox::OpenEditor(false)'s minimize (no caller passes false); legacy's DirectShow-only fullscreen behaviour (no toolbar row by default, playing on open in fullscreen). Windows (Winix VM, this branch with W1's two Windows build fixes 4c78c9ea and 1880f1d3 taken; build job-b44bb3ba383b4e6caf927c3caade24c8): `hikari_ui_shell_tests` V5 functions with videoContextMenuAndSnapshots and themeSwitchRetintsEverySurface offscreen, 9 passed (job-8c04e1a6b3bc4c2d89d66533bae180df); `video_fullscreen_tests` 6 passed (job-c53f8e3f024642a5b944c56e1268b212); the Spix workflow on the desktop (windows platform, task video-fullscreen-window) with both QXL monitors at 1024x768: every step and check passed, fullscreen on DISPLAY1 then DISPLAY2 with the dock, geometry and focus restored (job-4a5c06bca19648858f2ab5304d48aa2d). Native gate (gate_windows.py step `videofs`, local evidence out/v5/windows/gate), real keys through SendInput: F, Space, Left and Esc observed (Left changes the fullscreen monitor's pixels; the slow guest can play the clip to its last frame, where Right has none to go to); the context menu's monitor 2 observed at 125 % and at 150 % (1920x1200) with GetDpiForWindow equal to the monitor's (120, 144), the docked video hidden meanwhile and back after Esc with the focus on the Video panel. Not yet: Windows CI on the merged head; the Spix workflow at 150 %; the Windows runs again after the review's fixes (the menu's width, the volume slider shown, no title of its own; the Wayland placement does not apply there). Approved departure, 2026-10-06: [V5-open-editor-now](compatibility-decisions.md) (Open editor from fullscreen in the player layout turns the editor on at once; legacy set EDITOR_ON only, so the player layout stayed until the next start; the rewrite's switch is that setting). Linux runs at w5-video-gaps (2026-10-13, after the review fixes): the 57 `hikari_ui_*` offscreen suites here (view_arrangements and settings_import timed out under host load at -j3 and passed when rerun alone) and the other 1381 tests on akari-rvm (`hikari_ui_video_fullscreen_workflow` .xvfb, .twoscreens and .secondprimary among them) all passed, 9 of them skipped (PortAudio tests without a device, the third-party corpus).
