Follow from the masked field, approved (User, 2026-10-13; [compatibility-decisions.md](compatibility-decisions.md)): E4-time-field-ctrl-x, Ctrl+X copies the whole time as Ctrl+C and removes nothing (legacy's fell through to the text control's cut and broke the form; `shell_tests` timeFieldsKeepTheirForm); E4-time-field-mask-reverts, an edit the mask does not make (Ctrl+Backspace, the context menu's Cut, Delete or a partial Paste, a middle-click paste) is put back with the bell (`shell_tests` timeFieldsKeepTheirForm, timeFieldsPutBackEditsTheMaskDoesNotMake, timeFieldsMaskEachFormat). Also from the masked field: a number field holds nine digits (legacy's 20 overflowed wxAtoi) and its paste takes digits only (legacy's IsNumber let a sign through); Backspace's zeroed digits are sent when the field applies, as every changed field is (legacy's SetValue left IsModified unset, so Send kept the old time unless something else was typed); the bell is the field's `bell` signal, without a sound (as VisualHost::bell). Kept legacy behaviour: the Duration field keeps the typed text until SetLine (or, E4-esc-resets-duration, Esc); the Actor and Effect lists are rebuilt only on a Document switch and after an editor commit that changed them; switching Time/Frames drops time edits not yet sent (OnChangeTimeDisplay re-reads the Line); the switch is enabled once a video was opened and stays so; frames show only on an exact timebase; with several Lines selected every sent cell, the text included, goes to each of them. Differences: the Duration moves End whatever the live-editing option (E4-duration-live-off, user-approved 2026-10-05; legacy changed nothing with live editing off, EditBox.cpp:576-580); an applied time field shows the Line's form again; Esc's draft discard also clears the time fields' warning colour, a consequence of the accepted Esc discard (2026-09-29, edit-transactions.md), not a row of its own: the discard shows the Line's times again as legacy SetLine did, which reset the colour (EditBox.cpp:394-402; legacy had no Esc discard, and OnEdit cleared the colour only once Start was no later than End, otherwise Send or SetLine did); the Grid's warning colour is the palette's bright text (K2 reroutes it). Ported (User, 2026-10-13): the text field's colours and the comment/template/code state (TextEditor::SetState, DrawFieldD2D; Hikari's ASS highlighting of ass-editor.md, a comment's spelling marks off); TimeCtrl's mouse wheel and right-button drag on the clock forms, and the wheel on MicroDVD's frame number; the warning colour of times set from the audio display (AudioDisplay.cpp:1718-1722, 2825-2826); OnCommit's Send keeping that colour while a visual tool is on (EditBox.cpp:973). Ported (User, 2026-10-13, evening decisions 3 and 6): the modified field (E4-wheel-until-send, [compatibility-decisions.md](compatibility-decisions.md)): the wheel over a time field, the Layer or a margin without the focus changes only that field, which the next Send takes in Send's order and showing another Line, Esc, the Time/Frames switch or a time from the audio display drops (the editor's `timeStepped` and `numberStepped`); TimeCtrl's mouse on the number forms, reading the Document's format (TimeField.qml `mouseForm`); NumCtrl's wheel and right-button drag on the Layer and the margins (NumberField.qml), with no context menu from the right button (kept by the user's 2026-10-14 decision, item 20). Fixed (User, 2026-10-14, item 18; [compatibility-decisions.md](compatibility-decisions.md)): legacy's number-field mouse quirks, which the 2026-10-13 port had kept: the number forms step the number shown, one a notch or a drag step (E4-microdvd-mouse: MicroDVD's drag stepped no frame but 0 to 1, and the wheel down to 0 showed the frame it came from; E4-mpl2-mouse: MPL2's wheel down showed the same number and its drag took 100 steps a decisecond; E4-frames-mouse: the Time/Frames switch's wheel stepped the hidden time and left the number, its drag set the frame number to milliseconds), and NumCtrl's wheel and drag clamp to the range and show the clamped value (E4-numctrl-clamp: legacy's wheel showed nothing past the range and kept counting). The wheel over these fields does not scroll the editor panel, as legacy's fields took the wheel. Confirmed (User, 2026-10-14, items 16, 19 and 21): E4-wheel-until-send in every configuration, the step dropped by another Line or Esc, and a time from the audio display committing only that timing. Approved departures (User, 2026-10-14): a stepped field, focused or not, shows the theme's warning colour in its text and outline until it is sent or dropped, and says so in its accessible description and tooltip (E4-stepped-field-warning, item 17; legacy had no cue: only SetTime(..., true) from the audio display coloured a field); Esc's discard shows End - Start in Duration again and makes every field, the focused one included, drop its typed or stepped text and its modified flag (E4-esc-resets-duration, item 21). Differences kept: a step dropped by showing a Line is never written back (legacy's modified flag outlived SetLine, so its next Send wrote the shown time back, which changed a Line only under the frames switch, snapping it to the frame); the audio display's Send (AUDIO_CHANGE_TIME) takes the times it set, not a stepped Layer or margin, which wait for the editor's next Send, as the rewrite's audio commit leaves the draft apart; with the Time/Frames switch on and no exact timebase the fields show times and their mouse steps them as times (legacy's mouse read them as frame numbers); part of a notch is no step (legacy marked the field modified with a step of 0). Apply changes with nothing to send does nothing (E4-apply-nothing-to-send, user decision 2026-10-13, option B): legacy OnCommit ran SetVisual(true) and the audio display's SetDialogue (EditBox.cpp:980-983) even then; with something sent, the draft's new revision resets the visual tool and re-selects the audio box's Line (code reading, not tested). The dark and light editor colours are legacy's and not contrast-checked on the Qt fields (the visual review of all four themes is still open). Not yet: the Line number label and the translator-mode box of the counters row (E5). Approved departure [E6-ime-esc-list-first](compatibility-decisions.md) (User, 2026-10-16, decision 60, K8-K10/J5): an Escape that comes with an input method's end of composition ends it and closes the tag list, keeping the draft; the no-list case (K10's composition) changes the same way, recorded in the row; observed with a real IME after the change (same session: Esc while composing with the list open closed the list and kept the draft, the Microsoft Korean IME finalising the syllable; a second Esc reverted). Approved departure [E6-tag-list-raw-view](compatibility-decisions.md) (User, 2026-10-12): the tag list works in the raw view (tags shown) only: the Line editor's hidden-tag view, its default since V2 (the accepted ass-editor contract), refuses braces and backslashes as ASS syntax (MapRefusal::AssSyntax), so no override tag can be typed there to complete; legacy's editor had no hidden view and always showed the raw text, which the "Hide tags" switch off gives. The rewrite adds the dot/ring shape so the changed-Line mark does not rest on colour alone (subtitle-grid.md), an approved departure (E6-mark-shape, user 2026-10-05, wave-5 batch-3 review); the mark takes palette roles now and must take K2's Theme roles once K2 is merged. |

keyboard reach from the Grid (Shift+Tab to its tab, Right, Space, Escape back to the button, F6 on to the Reference tray); a double-click floating the Audio panel and docking it from its floating header; the bottom panels' one-tab headers with the tray's toolbar in its header and working, no close button there, the move cursor on the tab and the toolbar's free part and the arrow on the buttons, a right click on the toolbar's label or a button opening the tray's menu, a double-click on the label floating and docking the tray; every panel at least its minimum in the default arrangement and, squeezed from every side as far as the separators go, no control cut off (a check that does find cut-off controls when the engine is told a smaller minimum); the header colours in all four themes, the focused group's mark on its header, the named header controls through the accessibility tree; the audio box keeps 170 px with the Line editor at its minimum. The tests and the Winix VM gate cover them. The history below is kept. Windows real hardware (2026-10-15, branch `evidence/win-hw-20261015`, `d3/results.md`, comment on #207): free-part move, logical size across DPI, resize, Snap (still not taking the tool window, so on 2026-10-15 the owner made the whole header the docking drag on Windows: Snap is no longer offered there by design) and title-drag docking observed; monitor removal (item 8) failed, the floating Audio coming back at its size with only its left 173 px on the remaining screen (or, once, as a window larger than its panel). Fixed on branch `fix/d3-monitor-removal` (the test above fails without the fix: 600x220 at x 627 on an 800-px screen); run so far only in a local harness (the real docking.cpp and Dock QML with the theme singletons stubbed, Windows offscreen: `docking_qualification_tests` 30 passed, 1 X11-only skip), not yet in CI nor re-observed on the hardware. Native gate not yet re-run: Linux (sway, KWin, mutter, Hyprland, X11) and Windows for the borderless floating windows' move and resize (on each: the title's drag to dock with the highlight, a drag-and-drop on Wayland, and on Wayland and X11 the free part's system move, with the window manager's or compositor's edge tiling noted where they take the borderless tool window, on Windows the drag from right of the title docking with the highlight; the window system's resize from the shadow; the free part's move is new on X11 and Windows since 2026-10-12), the drawn shadow (Windows newly transparent and frameless; X11 without a compositor), focus and activation, drag-to-dock with the highlight on real pointers, mixed DPI and monitor removal. Decision on the move (D3-engine-move, [compatibility-decisions.md](compatibility-decisions.md)): the user chose on 2026-10-12 to split the header everywhere, so on X11 as on Wayland the title or tabs are the engine's drag (it docks, with the drop highlight) and the free part of the header uses startSystemMove (the window system's snapping and tiling; it does not dock); on 2026-10-15 the owner made the whole header the engine's drag on Windows, where Snap never takes the tool window; resize stays startSystemResize everywhere ([docking.md](docking.md#chrome)) Review: the user's approve or reject of these images on the review page; their Windows counterparts from the VM. Windows Snap: until 2026-10-15 Snap did not take the borderless floating window during the free part's move (native gate `floating-snap` at 9aa897065: left and top edges, not snapped, still floating; user decision 50); since 7812c180f the whole header is the engine's drag on Windows, so Snap is not offered there by design (D3-windows-snap), and the gate's `snap` step expects the engine's drag to the edges (the window follows the pointer, not snapped, still floating). |

MKV-attached fonts: legacy loaded a video's attachments into neither renderer (Demux::GetFontList and SaveFont serve only the font collector's "Demux fonts from loaded MKV file", FontCollector.cpp:954-969; SubtitlesLibass sets no ass_set_extract_fonts and adds no font); the rewrite does the same (Y9), so attachments reach a renderer only once written to the external fonts folder, as leases. Font-resolution read-back (branch w5-misc-gaps, 2026-10-13): `VsfilterRecipe.ReadsBackTheFacesItDrewWith` redirects the loaded xy-VSFilter module's import of gdi32!SelectObject and, for every font the renderer itself selects into a DC while it draws (CMyFont's metrics, RTS.cpp:131, and CText's outlines, :650), reads back the LOGFONT it asked for and the face GDI chose on that DC (GetTextFace and the outline metrics' full name); Arial, Arial bold italic and Times New Roman must be drawn with themselves, a missing family with a substitute reported as substituted, (vsfilter-drawn-faces.txt); `VsfilterRecipe.SelectsTheFacesTheLegacyReleaseSelects` (skipped without HIKARI_LEGACY_CSRI) compares, for the probe's families only, what each renderer asked for and the face GDI gave it against the legacy release's renderer (vsfilter-drawn-faces-legacy.txt). The redirect covers only the renderer module's own import table: xy-VSFilter links MFC statically and delay-loads only oleacc.dll, so its font selections should pass through it; if they reach gdi32 another way the read-back fails ("does not import gdi32!SelectObject by name" or no face read), it does not pass. Built and run only in windows-x64-vsfilter: not yet run (the import patch was syntax-checked here against Wine's headers only). Open: the shell suites in a full windows-x64-vsfilter run (only the W2 functions ran), a desktop observation of the switch in the running program (winix task `w2-switch-desktop`, added 2026-10-13, not yet run: `subtitleDisplayFilterSwitchesOnTheLoadedVideo` under the windows platform with HIKARI_SURFACE_SHOT_DIR saves the screen under the video picture after each switch (the grab in screen coordinates, taken once two grabs 250 ms apart agree) and requires it to change with xy-VSFilter and to come back unchanged with libass; the program itself, started from the desktop with the Options dialog, is still a later visual step), the Video page's dark screenshot on Windows again after the merge onto K2 (the windows dark shot on this card's base shows the native Windows Quick Controls style's light combo and button fields under the dark palette's light text, for every combo and button on the page; K2's HikariStyle, c3f56f52, replaces that style); Windows package (Winix VM, job-4ecc104d; after the review fixes job-882a79ff, the same): 1724 files, Csri/xy-VSFilter_hikarisub.dll 330ba69a... and Csri/VSFiltermod.dll 87a9c3c1... on Windows each end checks the other's process: its session, token SID and integrity level, which must be this one's or above, and each integrity level has its own instance, so an unelevated process hands an elevated HikariSub nothing); a later start hands its files (absolute; switches and `file:` URLs handled) and `XDG_ACTIVATION_TOKEN` over and exits, a start without files only brings the window forward, a crashed holder's lock and socket never block the next start, and a holder that does not answer within 5 s leaves the start to run alone (a start that runs alone, an update's watched start included, becomes the instance once the holder has gone), and the files are handed over only when the holder receipts the start's commit (a holder that quits, crashes or is held up takes nothing, so no file opens twice or is lost; a video or audio through `openSingleVideo`, keyframes in a new tab too); files a quit leaves are saved for the session's next start (`HandedOn-<session>.txt`, through an update's relaunch too; when they cannot be saved, or HikariSub runs elevated on Windows, whose unelevated programs can write that folder, on the next start's command line; on Windows a ready update then waits for the next quit, since its setup's start takes no files), and a start without files that came during the quit review that went ahead gets a new start; a start with several files opens them all and one whose file is a script or keyframes opens it as handed over (Automation, the tab); the window un-minimizes and raises the window (Windows: `AllowSetForegroundWindow` from the forwarding start; Wayland: the xdg-activation token). The installer's `Global\io.github.altqx.HikariSub.Running` mutex is unchanged and still held by every window ([distribution.md](distribution.md)); departures [WS5-forward-new-tab and WS5-activate-without-files](compatibility-decisions.md) | Linux (w6-ws5 at 2bf5da97, 2026-10-19; | [A11Y1](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/298) | Screen readers on GNOME and the other GTK desktops (Cinnamon, MATE, XFCE, Pantheon, Unity, LXDE). Where Qt has its GTK3 platform theme (build trees; the packages leave `qgtk3` out, cmake/HikariInstall.cmake), the theme starts GTK, and GTK 3's ATK bridge registers the process on the accessibility bus a second time: `hikarisub`, toolkit gtk, without HikariSub's windows, listed ahead of Qt's `HikariSub`, which holds the whole tree. It holds GTK's own dialogs, the theme's file chooser for every Qt Quick FileDialog and FolderDialog. Orca reads HikariSub and that chooser; a lookup that takes the first application named after HikariSub finds the empty one (the SH1 gate's "no windows" on mutter). The bridge stays on (owner, 2026-10-21): with `NO_AT_BRIDGE=1` Orca reads nothing of the chooser. No change to the application. | `native_gate_lib` test_the_lookup_finds_qt_s_application (`gatelib.hikarisub_application`: Qt's application ahead of GTK's) and test_the_gnome_session_keeps_gtk_s_bridge (the SH1 title bar step's mutter session and step `gtkbridge` set no `NO_AT_BRIDGE=1`). Native gate step `gtkbridge` (tools/native-gate/README.md): on mutter with GNOME's desktop names and on KWin with Plasma's, the platform theme, every application the process registered (`atspi_tool.py apps PID`), the lookup finding the main window and the core panels, and Orca reading File > Open's dialog and HikariSub after it. Research (qtbase 6.11.2 `qgenericunixtheme.cpp`, `qgtk3theme.cpp`; GTK 3.24 `gtkmain.c`, `a11y/gtkaccessibility.c`; at-spi2-core `atk-adaptor/bridge.c`) on the card |

| [KA7](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/282) | Karaoke checks: Subtitles > Check karaoke (after Check spelling) and the rewrite's own Global command `GLOBAL_CHECK_KARAOKE` (5172, no key by default, [editor shortcuts](editor-shortcuts.md)) open the modeless Karaoke check window (owner's placement choice on #282), a resizable window of its own on DG1's dialog frame that keeps its place and size (PK1: no in-window popups), Re-check at the footer's leading edge and Close at its trailing one. It lists the editing target's problems by Line: an unclosed syllable (a `{` with a karaoke tag that never closes, or `\k` with no number), a karaoke Line the audio editor does not read as one (none of its tags has its number at once, as in `\k50`: AudioKaraoke's legacy `\kf?o?[0-9]` test, so `\k+50` or `\k(50)` alone would be split anew), syllables the audio editor reads otherwise (once its test reads the Line as karaoke, AudioKaraoke's split splits at every `\k` or `\K` in a block as written and reads the number with legacy atoi up to the next `}` or `\`, so `\keep` and `\kfo50` are syllables to it and `\k(50)` is one of 0; a Kelvin sign U+212A for the K, a `k` to its QChar-lower-cased test, makes it read a Line with no karaoke at all; a tag with no number and a block that never closes are left to the unclosed check), syllables that don't add up to the Line's duration, zero-length syllables and syllables outside the Line (starting before it, ending after it, or running backward with a negative length), each listing at most its first 20 syllables and then "…", so a Line of millions of tags still makes short rows; the syllables are read one at a time, never all held. The window lists the first 1000 problems and says how many more there are ("N more problems not listed; fix these and check again"), so a Document of a great many bad Lines still makes a short list. `\k`, `\K`, `\kf` and `\ko` count, `\kt` does not; a value may be signed or sit in parentheses (whitespace before the number inside them is skipped, as atoi skips it), and other tags starting with k (`\keep`) are not karaoke, as the tag parser reads them; a fraction is dropped, as the karaoke the application plays drops it; the Line's duration is its end less its start in whole milliseconds, each truncated as the audio display's karaoke reads them; template, code and mixin Lines are skipped; a translated Line is checked on its translation; the editor's pending draft is checked as it stands, without committing it. Choosing a row selects its Line while that Document is still the editing target and the editor can leave its Line (otherwise, as for a draft it cannot commit, which it reports, the window checks again); Re-check reads the Document again; a clean Document says "No karaoke problems found". | `hikari_core_karaoke_checks_tests`: each check on the sample `tests/fixtures/karaoke/checks.ass` (one Line per check, plus a template Line, a timed pause and other tags that pass), signed, fractional, parenthesised and oversized values, syllables the audio editor reads otherwise, and translated Lines. `shell_tests` karaokeCheckWindowSelectsEachProblemsLine: the menu item and the command open the window, each check's row selects its Line, Re-check and the empty state. `hotkeys_tests`: the command's id, name and list row. Running the checks over the KA6 imports of twelve real files finds only their genuine zero-length syllables (output on #282). Linux (offscreen) and Windows MSVC CI. | None. |

## Maintaining the ledger

Each future surface ticket should name the relevant inventory rows and action families, link a runnable immutable prototype plus run instructions, capture the user's decision and point to its written UX outcome. Record any explicitly approved merge/drop and its reason. Do not count a placeholder panel, shared component gallery, proposed placement worksheet or successful syntax check as a reviewed complete workflow.

Before closing the map, re-check every row against the accepted written specification and final implementation-plan issues. The present ledger deliberately exposes missing work; it does not shrink the destination to the artifacts already available.
